# Connecticut — Election Directives, Guidance & Procedure Manuals

Dataset: State Election Rules and Guidance. Official guidance content for Connecticut, compiled for the VB Election Desk knowledge base. Every section below cites the official source it was captured from and the publisher's own currency statement; sources were verified as official publications before inclusion.
Sections: 37

### LEAD Communication (April 24, 2026) — Primary Petition Letter

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/april/2026-04-24-primary-petition-letter.pdf?rev=a71c282cee2a4ed69c727e87b5441489&hash=AFCE3423274135CC9DADCFD105A9726C. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Primary Petition Letter", April 24, 2026.*

Office of Secretary of the State 
State of Connecticut 
165 Capital Ave., Ste. 1000, P.O. Box 150470, Hartford, 
CT 06115-0470 
Stephanie Thomas 
Secretary of the State 
 
Jennifer D. Barahon 
Deputy Secretary of the State 
 
April 24, 2026 
To All Registrars of Voters: 
Re: Primary Petition Forms: State Offices, Municipal Offices At-Large and Municipal Offices From a 
Political Subdivision. 
 
In accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-410, we are enclosing two sets of Primary Petition Signature Pages and 
Instruction Pages that are prescribed for use in connection with candidacies for nomination to state and 
municipal offices. The form for "Municipal Offices at Large" is to be used for municipal offices for which all 
the electors of a particular municipality may vote. A list of these offices appears at the end of the applicable 
Instruction Page. 
 
The form for "Municipal Offices from Political Subdivisions" is to be used for those offices for which the 
electors of only part of a particular municipality may vote, a list of which appears at the end of the Instruction 
Page. 
 
We urge you to keep the enclosed forms for your file. They are the official forms prescribed under Conn. Gen. 
Stat. §9-410, and should not be altered in any way when preparing and providing primary petition forms for 
municipal office. If such pages are duplicated, they must be copied front to back as they appear in this 
packet. 
 
Pursuant to Conn. Gen. Stat. §9 -409, petition forms must be available from the registrar "beginning on the day 
following the making of the party's endorsement of a candidate or candidates for such office or position or 
beginning on the day following the final day for the making of such endorsement under the provisions of Conn. 
Gen. Stat. §9-391, whichever comes first." 
 
Please note the requirement under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9 -409 that a primary petition candidate for the municipal 
office of State Representative (chosen from a district composed of a single town or part of a single town) must 
include on their statement of consent the candidate’s "name as he authorizes it to appear on the ballot." 
(The enclosed primary petitions are not used for senatorial and assembly districts that cross town lines; for 
example, they are not used for a district composed of parts of two towns. Those petition pages are available 
from the Secretary of the State.) 
Please remember to complete the blank on each instruction page, relating to the number of signatures required, 
the petition due date, and Part A of each primary petition signature page, prior to issuing the same. The 
deadline for filing primary petitions is 4:00 p.m. on June 09, 2026. Please remember that all Registrars of 
Voters' Offices must be open between 1:00 p.m. and 4:00 p.m. on June 09, 2026. (Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-405). 
 
Under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9 -412, upon receiving the petition pages, the registrar must give to the person 
submitting them a signed receipt stating the number of pages and the date and time of filing. It also requires that 
after certifying on each page the number of signers who are enrolled electors, the registrar must file the pages 

with the town clerk, together with the registrar’s certificate as to the whole number of names on the last- 
completed enrollment list of such party in such town or political subdivision. 
 
Active and Inactive Enrollment List: 
 
In accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §§9-35, 9-54, 9-55, 9-64a, 9-64b and 9-396, there is an Active and Inactive 
enrollment list for each party. The Inactive list consists of persons who failed to respond to the Confirmation of 
Voting Residence (ED-642). We remind you that under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9 -35, Confirmation of Voting 
Residence Notices may not be sent out between May 1, 2026, and January 1, 2027, except that under Conn. Gen. 
Stat. §9-23g(c), if a Notice of Acceptance of a mail-in application card is returned undelivered, a Confirmation 
of Voting Residence Notice is sent out throughout the year.  
 
We strongly recommend that before the endorsement period, you prepare and print an Active and Inactive 
enrollment list for each party, and certify and file one with the town clerk. If municipal offices are endorsed by 
caucus, we also strongly recommend that in accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-42 and Conn. State Agencies 
Regs. §9-42-1, a registrar, deputy registrar, or assistant registrar of voters attends each caucus with an Active 
and Inactive enrollment list and voter registration applications for the purpose of restoring people from the 
Inactive to the Active enrollment list before they vote in the caucus. After the caucuses, if any, the registrar, 
deputy, or assistant registrar of voters should file in your office all restorations signed by the enrolled electors 
and the registrar, deputy, or assistant registrar. (Only one official is required to restore, except on election day 
when both registrars must consent.) We remind you, however, that under Conn. Gen. Stat. §§9 -56 and 9-57, 
people who file an application for enrollment on the day of a caucus do not acquire enrollment privileges until 
after the caucus. 
 
The number of signatures required on a primary petition is specified on the instruction sheet for each 
petition. When checking signatures on primary petitions, the signatures of all people who have enrollment 
privileges in the party at the time when the petition pages are reviewed must be counted. Under Conn. Gen. 
Stat. §9-35c, if an elector on the Inactive enrollment list signs a petition and gives the same address as appears 
on the Inactive list, you must forthwith place their name on the Active list and count the signature (no 
restoration form is necessary in such case). 
 
We are enclosing the following handouts, which provide helpful related information: 
Remove from Inactive List 
Petition Signatures on Inactive List 
If you have any questions, please call us at (860) 509-6100. 
Sincerely, 
 
 
Stephanie Thomas 
Secretary of the State 
 
 
 
By    
Bernard Liu 
 Staff Attorney  
Enclosures: ED-619, ED-619a, 2 Handouts Information copies to: 
Town Clerks

### LEAD Communication (June 23, 2026) — 2026 Notice of Primary to Town Clerks

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/june/2026-notice-of-primary-to-town-clerks.pdf?rev=cca42f88dddc4a37a93e2c6c8d14375a&hash=3B7B573D8D178E44E20D22190DE5B866. To the Municipal Clerk: June 23, 2026 
 
Connecticut General Statutes Section 9-433 requires the Secretary of the State to notify 
the Municipal Clerk of each town within the state or district when a primary is to be held 
for nomination of a political party’s candidate for office. 
 
The statute provides that, "the clerk of each such town shall thereupon cause such notice 
to be published forthwith in a newspaper having a general circulation in such town, or towns 
in the case of joint publication under [C.G.S. § 9-433(b)], together with a statement of the 
date upon which the primary is to be held, the hours during which the polls shall be open, 
and the location of the polls." 
 
Therefore, we are enclosing a Notice of Republican and Democratic Primary for the 
parties as well as the state and multi-town district offices indicated therein. Additionally 
enclosed is the suggested form of the statement concerning the date, polling hours, and 
polling locations for the primary. Not all district offices that are listed on the Notice of 
Primary apply to your town. Select the offices and candidates that do apply before preparing 
your statements to be published and ballots to be printed. Also, before publishing the 
enclosed statements, you must add any primaries for municipal offices that you may 
be having, as determined by your registrars of voters, and complete the statement by filling 
in the location of the polling places. 
 
If you are  having multiple primaries for the same party , you may publish one 
consolidated notice for that party. List the endorsed and non-endorsed candidates for each 
office following the format of the enclosed notices and listing the offices in the order set by 
C.G.S. § 9-251 and described below. Separate notices must be published for each 
party. 
 
Pursuant to C.G.S. § 9-251, list offices, if applicable, as follows: Governor and Lieutenant 
Governor, United States senator, representative in Congress, state senator, state 
representative, Secretary of the State, Treasurer, Comptroller, Attorney General, and judge 
of probate.  
 
In compliance with C.G.S. § 9-435, you will receive notice of any primary for a municipal 
office in your town from the party's local Registrar of Voters. In the case of primaries for 
different municipal offices within the same party, a consolidated notice may be published 
if desired. Again, separate notices must be published for each party.  
 
Please Note: When preparing ballots, please remember that the order and format must 
comply with C.G.S. § 9-251 and C.G.S. § 9-437. Also note that the names of the candidates 

 
2 | P a g e  
 
are to be printed as they appear, and in the order in which they are listed, in the enclosed 
notices. The "ballot versions" of the names are legally authorized on candidate filings with 
this office. Pursuant to C.G.S.§ 9-437, the name of the party-endorsed candidate for 
each office is to be designated by an asterisk (*) on the ballots. If there is no party-
endorsed candidate, the words "No Party Endorsement" shall appear immediately beneath 
the office designation at the head of the appropriate vertical office column.  
 
We appreciate your cooperation and assistance. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Stephanie Thomas 
Secretary of the State 
 
By:   
Erin Flaharty 
Staff Attorney

### LEAD Communication (February 5, 2026) — Permanent Absentee Balloting

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/february/2026-permanent-ab-status-mailing.pdf?rev=90a0fbbe8d76476bb7cd0955bf9167dd&hash=49CBE24036F38CDC42254010ACDD2A14. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Permanent Absentee Balloting", February 5, 2026.*

Office of the Connecticut Secretary of the State 
165 Capitol Avenue 
Hartford, CT 06106 
 
 
 
To: All Town Clerks and Registrars of Voters 
From: The Legislation and Elections Administration Division 
Date: February 5, 2026 
Re: Permanent Absentee Balloting 
As you know, the law establishes permanent absentee ballot status for people who qualify. Below, 
we explain the process and provide details on the procedure, including eligibility  and the annual 
notice. 
Overview 
The law makes electors with a permanent disability, or suffering from a long-term illness, eligible 
for permanent absentee ballot status (CGS Sec. 9 -14e, as amended by PA 24-148, Sec. 16). Once 
attained, this status remains with the voter until they (1) are removed from the permanent 
absentee ballot list pursuant to the process outlined below, ( 2) are removed from  the town's 
official registry list, or (3) request to be removed from permanent absentee ballot status.  This 
status enables the elector to receive an absentee ballot for each election, primary, and 
referendum in the municipality in which they are eligible to vote. 
Please note that pursuant to PA 24-148, Sec. 16, registrars can no longer remove an elector from 
the permanent absentee ballot status list because they fail to return the annual notice.  The 
reasons for which registrars can remove an elector from the list are outlined below. 
Eligibility 
To be eligible for permanent absentee ballot status, an elector must file an absentee ballot 
application, together with a doctor's certificate stating that they have a permanent disability or 
long-term illness and are unable to appear in person at their po lling place. The law does not 
prescribe an official form for use as a doctor’s certificate. Therefore, a note from the elector’s 
doctor (presumably, on letterhead) which indicates  a disability or long -term illness and the 
statement that the elector is unable to appear in person at the polls satisfies this requirement. 
Procedure 
When a municipal clerk receives an absentee ballot application accompanied by a doctor’s 
certificate from an elector seeking absentee ballot status, the clerk should forward a copy of the 

application to the registrars of voters so they can indicate permanent status on the elector’s 
record in the centralized voter registration system (CVRS). 
The municipal clerk must send out an absentee ballot to any elector with the status for each event 
in which they are eligible to vote. Clerks should send absentee ballots on the first day when they 
become available (e.g., 21 days before a primary and 31 days before an election).  CGS Sec. 9 -
140(f). Although permanent absentee ballot status allows the municipal clerk to send out an 
absentee ballot for each relevant event without an additional absentee ballot application, it does 
not allow the municipal clerk to forgo any other absentee balloting requirements.  This means, 
for example, that clerks must include all the necessary envelopes in the absentee voting sets and 
comply with supervised absentee balloting requirements. 
We asked the Connecticut Freedom of Information Commission (FOIC) whether the doctor’s 
certification is subject to disclosure pursuant to a Freedom of Information Act (FOIA) request. 
FOIC indicated that the doctor’s note is not subject to disclosure.  The original absentee ballot 
application, however, is subject to disclosure just like any other absentee ballot application. As a 
reminder, CGS Sec. 9 -140(i) states, “ the municipal clerk shall file executed applications in 
alphabetical order according to the applicants' surnames. Such applications shall be preserved as 
a public record as required by section 9-150b.” 
Annual Notice to Determine Residency 
The registrars of voters must send an annual written notice in January of each year, on a form 
prescribed by the Secretary of the State, to determine if an elector with this status continues to 
reside at the address on their permanent absentee ballot appli cation. In response to this letter, 
the registrars of voters must perform one of four possible actions. The registrars must: 
1. remove the elector from permanent absentee ballot status if the notice is returned as 
undeliverable; 
2. remove the elector from the official registry list and send the elector a voter registration 
application if they have moved out of town; 
3. leave the elector on permanent absentee ballot status and change the elector’s address 
if they have moved within the municipality; or 
4. leave the elector on permanent absentee ballot status without changing their address if 
they have not moved. 
Please note: Under Public Act 24-148, Sec. 16, registrars can no longer remove electors from the 
permanent absentee ballot status list because they fail to return the annual notice . Similarly, 
registrars cannot remove electors from the official registry simply for failure to return the notice. 
We provide a sample notice at the end of this letter for registrars of voters to use when contacting 
an elector on the permanent absentee ballot list. 
Notice of Permanent Absentee Ballot Status 
Date: ______________________ 
 
Name:   
 
Address:   
 
 
Post Office Box: (optional) 
Date of Birth: (optional) 
 
Important! This Notice Concerns Your Permanent Absentee Ballot Status 
 
The Registrars of Voters are conducting an annual review as required by law to determine if you 
continue to reside at the residence address indicated above for purposes of your permanent 
absentee ballot status. The address listed above is the address you provided on your permanent 
absentee ballot application. If you would like to remove yourself from the permanent absentee 
ballot list, you may use this form to do so. 
Please check the statement below that applies, sign in the space provided below, and 
immediately return this form. 
 
☐ Remove me from the permanent absentee ballot list. 
☐ My residence is at the address shown above. 
☐ I have moved. The address of my new residence is: 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Note: If your new residence is in another town, you must register to vote in that town to be 
entitled to vote there. If your new residence address is still within this town, we will change 
your address on the official voter list. 
 
Please record this change on the voter registry list. 
 
 
Signature of Elector

(ED-619ST Rev. 4/24 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. At-Large) 
 
   
 
Instruction Page for Primary  Petition Page for Primary  Petition for State Office(s) 
Note: (1) Complete on this Instruction Page, in the space provided, the total number of 
signatures needed for a candidacy for the nomination to the particular state office(s); (2) 
Provide at least one instruction page for each circulator; (3) Provide one or more  Primary 
Petition Signatures Pages suitable for  duplication as you deem  necessary (however, if the  
candidate or  candidates are  indigent, provide at least two times the number of signature 
pages needed to contain the required number of signatures); and (4) Complete all blanks in 
Part A of each primary petition signature page provided.  
*******************************************************************************
* 
 
Request for Petition Forms : Any person who requests a petition form must give to the 
Secretary of the State before they may obtain the  form (1) their own name and address, and 
the name, address, and office sought of each candidate for whom the petition is being 
obtained; and (2) a statement signed by each such candidate that they consent to be a 
candidate for said office.  Each candidate must include on the statement of consent their 
name as they authorize it to appear on the ballot. After a person has completed the foregoing 
requirements, the Secretary of the State, before giving them the petition form, must fill in 
on each Petition Signatures Page the name and address of each candidate to be named 
therein, the office sought by each such candidate, the name of the political party holdi ng the 
primary, and the date of the primary, and must also fill in the blanks on this Instruction Page.  
 
Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be Provided : Upon completion of the above 
requirements, the Secretary of the State shall give to the person requesting the petition form 
one or more signature pages suitable for duplication as they deem necessary.  If the candidate 
or candidates are indigent, the Secretary shall provide a number of pages determined by the 
Secretary as at least two times the number sufficient to contain the required number of 
signatures. The number of signatures  required is  __ at least 2% of  the enrolled party 
members of the party in the state or such lesser number as the party rules prescribe.  
 
Time of Filing: This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party whose 
nomination is sought and in the town in which such petition was circulated not later than 
4:00 p.m. on June 09, 2026. 
 
General Information : A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a 
party- endorsed candidate) whose name appears on the last -completed enrollment list of the 
party within any  municipality in the State by filing the required petition with the registrar of 
the party. A petition may propose as many candidates for different offices as there are 
candidates to be nominated by the party statewide, but no more.  If duplicated, petition 
pages should be duplicated front to back  exactly as they appear in this packet.  
Misrepresentation Prohibited : It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally 
misrepresent the contents of a primary petition.  Conviction of a Class D felony shall be 
punished by imprisonment for a term of not less than one year nor more than five years, or 
a fine not exceeding $5,000, or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature 
of the signer; and one individual may not, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, 
sign for another.  Any person who signs a name other than their  own to a  primary 
petition or who  signs a name other  than their own as a circulator of such a petition 
shall be fined not  more than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one 
year or both. Signatures shall be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page 
distributed by the Secretary of the State or on  duplicates of the pages issued by the 
Secretary.  No person shall sign as a petition signer more than once for the same candidate 
or candidates.  Petition signatures may not be withdrawn.  Only the signatures of electors 
whose names appear upon the last-completed enrollment list of the party holding the primary 
in the municipality will be counted.  If the registrar determines that an enrolled elector on the 
inactive registry list signed a petition giving the same address  as on the inactive registry list, 
such name shall forthwith be placed on the active registry list and counted.  Signers who 
have moved within town should be encouraged to give their birth date because the registrar 
is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the street address on the petition is different 
from the street address on the enrollment list if (1) the  signer is eligible to vote for the 
candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown on the petition is the same 
as the birth date on the signer's registration record.  
 
 
(ED-619ST Rev. 4/24 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. At-Large) 
 
   
 
Assistance to People who are Blind in the Signing of Petitions : Any person who is 
blind may cause their name to be placed on a petition if  an authorized  agent (not the 
circulator) reads aloud the full text of  the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the 
blind person consents to having their name appear on it.  If a blind person is unable to write, 
their authorized agent may write the name of the blind person followed by the word "by" and 
the agent’s own signature.  
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party holding the primary 
and a United States citizen at least 18 years of age.  No person may circulate petitions for 
more than the maximum number of candidates to be nominated by a party for the same 
office.  No candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition 
for another candidate (or another group of candidates contained in one petition) for the 
nomination of the party for the same office.  A candidate may circul ate a primary petition in 
which he is proposed as a candidate.  Any petition page circulated in violation of these 
provisions of the law must be rejected by the registrar. The circulator must be certain to have 
signers sign in their presence, and that the circulator’s own signed statements are true, or 
the circulator s hall be guilty of  a Class A misdemeanor, and the  whole page may be rejected.  
A Class A misdemeanor is punishable by up to 364 days in prison, a fine of up to $2,000, or 
both. 
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member : 
Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the 
appropriate party attesting that the circulator has represented that they are an enrolled 
member of such party.  This statement may be completed at any time but must be completed 
before the petition page is  submitted for filing to the party's registrar in the municipality of 
voting residence of the signers. Unless such a statement by the reg istrar appears on each 
page submitted, the page must be rejected by the registrar.  
 
Circulator's Statement of  Authenticity of Signatures : The Circulator's Statement of  
Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be 
completed and signed by the circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing 
the page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters.  The page must be rejected by the registrar 
if the Statement is not completed  and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer : After signing the Circulator's Statement of 
Authenticity of Signatures, the circulator must appear before one of the following officers to 
acknowledge the statement:  a judge of a court of record, clerk  or deputy clerk of a  court 
having a seal, family support magistrate,  town clerk, notary public, justice of the peace, or 
attorney admitted  to the Connecticut Bar.  The acknowledgment must be completed and 
signed by the officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the registrar 
if this is not done.  
 
"Registrar" Defined : "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political 
party holding a primary and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for 
different voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at  the 
last-preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of 
the vote of the whole municipality was moderator.  
*************************************************************************
*** 
Offices: 
 
This primary petition form is  to be used in connection with the following state offices  
when applicable: 
 
1) Governor and Lieutenant Governor, 
2) Secretary of the State, 
3) Treasurer,  
4) Comptroller, 
5) Attorney General, and 
6) Congressional Districts. 
 
 
 
 
2 
(ED-619ST Rev. 4/24)

without legal authority to do so, and you may not sign this petition if you are 
not an elector.

Statement by Registrar  of Voters as to Circulator’s Status as Enrolled Party Member 
C. Circulator must have Part C filled in by party’s registrar before  submitting it to party's 
registrar in town in which the signers are enrolled.  
I, Registrar of Voters of the party named in Part A of this petition, in the Town of  , CT, do hereby 
attest that the above circulator represents themselves as an enrolled member of such party.

Instruction Page for Primary  Petition Page for Primary  
Petition for Municipal Office(s) At -Large 
Note: (1) Complete on this Instruction Page, in the space provided, the total number of 
signatures needed for a candidacy for the nomination to the particular state office(s); (2) Provide 
at least one instruction page for each circulator; (3) Provide one or more  Primary Petition 
Signatures Pages suitable for  duplication as you deem  necessary (however, if the  candidate or  
candidates are  indigent, provide at least two times the number of signature pages needed to 
contain the required number of signatures); and (4) Complete all blanks in Part A of each primary 
petition signature page provided.  
****************************************************************************
Request for Petition Forms : Any person who requests a petition form must give to the 
Secretary of the  State before they may obtain the  form (1) their own name and address, and 
the name, address, and office sought of each candidate for whom the petition is being 
obtained; and (2) a statement signed by each such candidate that they consent to be a 
candidate for said office.  Each such candidate must include on the statement of consent their 
name as they authorize it to appear on the ballot.  After a person has completed the foregoing 
requirements, the Secretary of the State, before giving them the petition form, must fill in on 
each Petition Signatures Page the name and address of each candidate to be named therein, 
the office sought by each such candidate, the name of the political party holding the primary, 
and the date of the primary, and must also fill in the blanks on this Instruction Page.  
 
Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be Provided : Upon completion of the above 
requirements, the Secretary of the State shall give to the person requesting the petition form 
one or more signature pages suitable for duplication as he deems necessary.  If the candidate or 
candidates are indigent, the Secretary shall provide a number of pages determined by the 
Secretary as at least two times the number sufficient to contain the required number of 
signatures. The number of signatures required is at least 5% of the electors whose names appear 
on the active last- completed enrollment list of the party in the municipality or such lesser number 
as the party rules prescribe. 
 
Time of Filing : This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party whose 
nomination is sought and in the town in which such petition was circulated not later than 4:00 
p.m. on June 09, 2026. 
General Information: A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a party - 
endorsed candidate) whose name appears on the last -completed enrollment list of the party 
within any municipality in the State by filing the required petition with the registrar of the party. 
A petition may propose as many candidates for different offices as there are candidates to be 
nominated by the party statewide, but no more.  If duplicated, petition pages should be 
duplicated front to back  exactly as they appea r in this packet. 
 
Misrepresentation Prohibited : It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally 
misrepresent the contents of a primary petition.  Conviction of a Class D felony shall be punished 
by imprisonment for a term of not less than one year nor more than five years, or a fine not 
exceeding $5,000, or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature of the 
signer; and one individual may not, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, sign for 
another. Any person who signs a name other than their  own to a primary petition or 
who signs a name other  than their own as a circulator of such a petition shall be fined 
not more than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one year or both. 
Signatures shall be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page distributed by the 
Secretary of the State or on  duplicates of the pages issued by the Secretary.  No person shall 
sign as a petition signer more than once for the same candidate or candidates. Petition signatures 
may not be withdrawn.  Only the signatures of electors whose names appear upon the last -
completed enrollment list of the party holding the primary in the munici pality will be counted.  If 
the registrar determines that an enrolled elector on the inactive registry list signed a petition 
giving the same address as on the inactive registry list, such name shall forthwith be placed on 
the active registry list and counted.  Signers who have moved within town should be encouraged 
to give their birth date because the registrar is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the 
street address on the petition is different from the street address on the enrollment list if (1)  the 
signer is eligible to vote for the candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown 
on the petition is the same as the birth date on the signer's registration record.  
(ED-619 - Rev. 4/24 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. At-Large) 
 
   
 
Assistance to People who are Blind in the Signing of Petitions : Any person who is blind 
may cause their name to be placed on a petition if  an authorized agent (not the circulator) reads 
aloud the full text of the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the blind person consents 
to having their name appear on it.  If a blind person is unable to write, their authorized agent 
may write the name of the blind person followed by the word "by" and the agent’s own signature.  
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party holding the primary and a 
United States citizen at least 18 years of age.  No person may circulate petitions for more than 
the maximum number of candidates to be nominated by a party for the same office.  No 
candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition for another 
candidate (or another group of candidates contained in one petition) for the nomination of the 
party for the same office.  A candidate may circul ate a primary petition in which he is proposed 
as a candidate. Any petition page circulated in violation of these provisions of the law must be 
rejected by the registrar. The circulator must be certain to have signers sign in their presence, 
and that the circulator’s own signed statements are true, or the circulator s hall be  guilty of  a 
Class A misdemeanor, and the  whole page may be rejected.  A Class A misdemeanor is 
punishable by up to 364 days in prison, a fine of up to $2,000, or both.  
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member : 
Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the appropriate 
party attesting that the circulator has represented that they are an enrolled member of such 
party. This statement may be completed at any time but must be completed before the petition 
page is submitted for filing to the party's registrar in the municipality of voting residence of the 
signers. Unless such a statement by the reg istrar appears on each page so submitted, the page 
must be rejected by the registrar.  
 
Circulator's Statement of  Authenticity of Signatures : The Circulator's Statement of  
Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be 
completed and signed by the circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing the 
page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters.  The page must be rejected by the registrar if the 
Statement is not completed  and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer : After signing the Circulator's Statement of 
Authenticity of Signatures, the circulator must appear before one of the following officers to 
acknowledge the statement:  a judge of a court of record, clerk or deputy clerk of a court having 
a seal, family  support magistrate, town clerk, notary public, justice of the peace, or attorney 
admitted to the Connecticut Bar.  The acknowledgment must be completed and signed by the 
officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the registrar if this is not 
done. 
 
"Registrar" Defined : "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political party 
holding a primary and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for different 
voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at  the last -
preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of the vote 
of the whole municipality was moderator.  
**************************************************************************** 
Offices: 
 
This primary petition form is  to be used in connection with the following municipal offices  when 
applicable: 
 
1. A town, city, or borough office  for which only and all electors of such town, city, 
or borough may vote;  
2. State Representative in an Assembly District composed of  a single town; 
3. Judge of Probate in a Probate District composed of a single 
town;  
4.Registrar of Voters, except where elected from  voting districts. 
(ED-619 - Rev. 4/24) 
 
   
 
Warning: It is a Crime to Sign this Petition in the Name of Another Person Without 
Legal Authority to do so, and you may not Sign this Petition if you are not an Elector.

Request for Petition Forms : Any person who requests a petition form must give to the

Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be Provided : Upon completion of the above

one or more signature pages suitable for duplication as he deems necessary.  If the candidate or

Secretary as at least two times the number sufficient to contain the required number of 
signatures. The number of signatures required is at least 5% of the electors whose names appear 
on the active last- completed enrollment list of the party in the municipality or such lesser number 
as the party rules prescribe. 
 
Time of Filing : This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party whose 
nomination is sought and in the town in which such petition was circulated not later than 4:00 
p.m. on June 09, 2026. 
 
General Information: A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a party - 
endorsed candidate) whose name appears on the last -completed enrollment list of the party 
within any municipality in the State by filing the required petition with the registrar of the party. 
A petition may propose as many candidates for different offices as there are candidates to be 
nominated by the party statewide, but no more.  If duplicated, petition pages should be 
duplicated front to back  exactly as they appear in this packet.  
 
Misrepresentation Prohibited : It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally 
misrepresent the contents of a primary petition.  Conviction of a Class D felony shall be punished 
by imprisonment for a term of not less than one year nor more than five years, or a fine not 
exceeding 
$5,000, or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature of the 
signer; and one individual may not, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, sign for 
another. Any person who signs a name other than their  own to a primary petition or 
who signs a name other  than their own as a circulator of such a petition shall be fined 
not more than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one year or both. 
Signatures shall be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page distrib uted by the 
Secretary of the State or on  duplicates of the pages issued by the Secretary.  No person shall 
sign as a petition signer more than once for the same candidate or candidates. Petition signatures 
may not be withdrawn.  Only the signatures of electors whose names appear upon the last -
completed enrollment list of the party holding the primary in the municipality will be counted.  If 
the registrar determines that an enrolled elector on the inactive registry list signed a petition 
giving the same address  as on the inactive registry list, such name shall forthwith be placed on 
the active registry list and counted.  Signers who have moved within town should be encouraged 
to give their birth date because the registrar is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the 
street address on the petition is different from the street address on the enrollment list if (1) the  
signer is eligible to vote for the candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown 
on the petition is the same as the birth da te on the signer's registration record.  
 
   
 
(ED-619-DIST - Rev. 4/24 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. Political Subdivision) 
 
Assistance to People who are Blind in the Signing of Petitions : Any person who is blind 
may cause their name to be placed on a petition if  an authorized agent (not the circulator) reads 
aloud the full text of the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the blind person consents 
to having their name appear on it.  If a blind person is unable to write, their authorized agent 
may write the name of the blind person followed by the word "by" and the agent’s own signature.  
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party holding the primary and a 
United States citizen at least 18 years of age.  No person may circulate petitions for more than 
the maximum number of candidates to be nominated by a party for the same office.  No 
candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition for another 
candidate (or another group of candidates contained in one petition) for the nomination of the 
party for the same office.  A candidate may circulate a primary petition in which he is proposed 
as a candidate. Any petition page circulated in violation of these provisions of the law must be 
rejected by the registrar. The circulator must be certain to have signers sign in their presence, 
and that the circulator’s own signed statements are true, or the circulator s hall be  guilty of  a 
Class A misdemeanor, and the  whole page may be rejected.  A Class A misdemeanor is 
punishable by up to 364 days in prison, a fine of up to $2,000, or bo th. 
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member : 
Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the appropriate 
party attesting that the circulator has represented that they are an enrolled member of such 
party. This statement may be completed at any time but must be completed before the petition 
page is submitted for filing to the party's registrar in the municipality of voting residence of the 
signers. Unless such a statement by the reg istrar appears on each page so submitted, the page 
must be rejected by the registrar.  
 
Circulator's Statement of  Authenticity of Signatures : The Circulator's Statement of  
Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be 
completed and signed by the circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing the 
page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters.  The page must be rejected by the registrar if the 
Statement is not completed  and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer : After signing the Circulator's Statement of 
Authenticity of Signatures, the circulator must appear before one of the following officers to 
acknowledge the statement:  a judge of a court of record, clerk or deputy clerk of a court having 
a seal, family  support magistrate, commissioner of deeds, town clerk, notary public, justice of 
the peace, or attorney admitted to the Connecticut Bar.  The acknowledgment must be completed 
and signed by the officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the registrar 
if this is not done.  
 
"Registrar" Defined : "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political party 
holding a primary and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for different 
voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at  the last -
preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of the vote 
of the whole municipality was moderator.

Remove from Inactive List to Off-List if: 
1. Death (9-35) 
2. Cancellation is received from  new place where voter registered (9-35) 
3. Voter writes "I moved out of town" (9-35) 
4. DMV writes "he moved out of town" (send Notice of Removal with new card) (9-35) 
Remove from Inactive List to Active List if: 
 
l. Restoration is signed and filed by voter (9-42) 
2. Voter re-registers in same town (even if different  address, but must retain old active list 
for four years to restore if made mistake).  
3. Voter signs petition giving same address (9-35c) 
4. DMV writes "he moved within town" (no notice mailed) (NVRA Sec. 5(d)) 
5. Voter writes "I moved within town" (NVRA Sec. 8(f)) 
 
Leave on Inactive List, unless Restoration Form filed if: 
 
l. Voter requests an Absentee Ballot (because voters think they have right to vote in former 
town by absentee ballot). (Inactive voters in the service can be restored as clerical error 
[9-40a, 9-42(a)]. Inactive voters out of the country can be directed an  absentee ballot 
and notified by town clerk that unless  restoration form  [a signed Federal Postcard 
Application {FPCA}qualifies as a restoration form] is received by election day, ballot will 
not be counted [9 -153d].) 
2. Voter signs petition with different address and no birth date (If they sign a primary or 
nominating petition with different  address and same birth date, count but do not restore 
to active list [9-35c, 9-412, 9-453k]) 
3. Voter shows up at caucus, primary, or election (in order to vote, the voter must fill out 
restoration form, indicating their latest address in town).  
 
   
 
From the Office of the Secretary  of the 
State 165 Capitol Avenue  
Hartford,  Connecticut 06106 
 
Key Reminders for Petition Signatures of Persons on Active and Inactive Lists 
Primary  Petitions (contain line for birthdate) 
 
1. If on active list and different  address and same birthdate; count and change address on 
enrollment list (9-412, 9-35) 
2. If on active list and different  address and no birthdate, research and try to count but 
do not change address on enrollment list without further reliable information.  
 
3. If on inactive list and same address, restore and count. (9-35c) 
4. If on inactive list and different address and same birthdate; count but do not restore to  
active list until voter  signs restoration (under penalties of false statement)  and do not 
change address on inactive list. (9- 412) 
 
5. If on inactive list and different address and no birthdate, cannot count. (The most you may 
do is send a Restoration  form with  the note, "If you are the voter who lived at this old 
address, sign this restoration form so you will be on active list in future.")  
 
Nominating Petitions (contain line for birthdate) 
1. If on active list and different  address and same birthdate on registration record; 
count and notify registrars to change address on registry list. (9 -453k, 9-35) 
 
2. If on active list and different  address and no birthdate, hunt up and try to count but 
notify registrars to not change address on registry list without further reliable information.  
3. If on inactive list and same address, count and notify registrar to restore to active. (9-35c) 
 
4. If on inactive list and different  address and same birthdate on registration record; 
count but notify registrars to not restore to active list until voter signs restoration 
(under penalties of false statement) and not change address on inactive list. (9 -453k) 
5. If on inactive list and different  address and no birthdate, can't count. (The most you 
could do is notify registrars to send a Restoration form with note "If you are the voter who 
lived at this old address, sign this restoration form so you will be on active list in future.")

### LEAD Communication (June 2026) — Absentee Ballot - Cure Eligible Letter

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/june/absentee-ballot---cure-eligible-letter.pdf?rev=cb5627af2c91442880994087b8d79b0e&hash=274A73A8366B0DCDB2DE8324470EE033. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. [Place on Town or Town Clerk letterhead] 
 
 
Date:  
RE:  Action Required: Signature Needed to Process Your Absentee Ballot 
 
Dear Voter, 
This letter is to inform you that the absentee ballot you submitted for the upcoming 
election, primary, or referendum cannot be processed because you did not sign your ballot 
return envelope as required by Connecticut law.  
Under Public Act 26-42, you have the right to cure this defect so that your absentee ballot 
may be counted. To cure the defect, you must appear in person at the Town Clerk’s office 
during normal business hours, no later than 8:00 p.m. on the day of the election, 
primary, or referendum, to provide your signature in your own handwriting. 
If the defect is not cured by the deadline, your absentee ballot will be rejected, and your 
vote will not be counted. 
Alternatively, the voter may vote in person during the early voting period or on election day. 
If you have any questions or need assistance, please contact the Town Clerk’s office at 
[phone number] or [email address]. We are committed to helping ensure your vote is 
counted. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Town Clerk 
[Municipality Name]

### LEAD Communication (June 2026) — Blank Absentee Ballot Mailing

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/june/blank_ab-mailing-2026.pdf?rev=c40cc9cad8874befa8118f00e34a72a9&hash=68B57F3B7DA745FD36A59EE9514EF7DE. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). June 8, 2026 
 
 
 
To Municipal Clerks of Municipalities Holding Primaries on August 11, 2026 and 
Elections on November 3, 2026: 
 
Re: Official Blank Absentee Ballots (ED-81pr and ED-81a) 
 
Active and Inactive Registry List: 
 
If you receive an application for an absentee ballot from a  member of the armed forces, spouse 
or dependent, or federal employee, and such person is on the inactive registry list, we advise that 
you ask the registrars of voters to immediately restore the name of such person to the active 
registry list as a clerical error under Conn. Gen. St at. §§9-40a and 9-42(a) and that they keep a 
record that they did so. 
 
If you receive an application for an absentee ballot from an unknown category of elector residing 
outside the United States and such person is on the inactive registry list, we advise that you (1) 
fill out an ED -12 Direction by Registrar form for such pers on, (2) send the blank ballot at the 
earliest allowable time, and (3) separately mail (a) an Application for Voter Registration for 
restoration purposes, (b) an ED-22 Envelope, and (c) a note that unless the Application for Voter 
Registration for restorati on purposes is returned separately in the ED -22 Envelope by election 
day (and the blank ballot is separately returned by 8:00 p.m. on election day), the absentee ballot 
will not be counted.  If you received an original, signed Federal Post Card Application (FPCA) 
requesting a full ballot, you may treat it as a restoration form. 
 
Official Blank Ballot: 
 
In this email, we have included the Official Blank Ballot for both the primary and the general 
election. 
 
A.  90-Day Military Contingency Blank Ballot 
 
Under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-153e, the Official Blank Absentee Ballot must be made available 
beginning August 5th, 2026, to members of the armed forces, and spouses or dependents 
living where such members are stationed, who state on their absentee ballot application that 
due to military contingencies they request an early ballot for the general election on 
November 3, 2026 (please note: the 90- day ballot is not available for special elections or 
primaries). This includes FPCAs bearing a sticker referring to mi litary contingencies, as 
follows: 
 
"Connecticut” 
" 'Due to military contingencies, I am unable to follow the regular 
absentee ballot application process.  I request a special write- in 
absentee ballot.' " 
 
To Municipal Clerks 
Page 2 
 
 
In addition, Section 9-153d permits registrars of voters and municipal clerks on Form ED-12 
to direct Official Blank Absentee Ballots, in such cases of military contingencies, for the 
general election on November 3, 2026, beginning August 5, 2026, to members of the armed 
forces and spouses and dependents living where the armed forces member is stationed.  The 
law relating to the military contingency blank ballot also requires that the municipal clerk 
provide with the blank ballot "a list of the offices to be voted upon indicating the number of 
individuals for which each elector may vote."  A blank ballot sent out before a complete list of 
candidates and questions is available must include a statement prepared by you that the 
complete list of candidates and questions is not available and will be mailed as soon as it 
becomes available.  See the sugge sted enclosed form ED -10.  Later when the list of 
candidates becomes available, you must mail the list to each person who was sent a blank 
ballot without such list.  If the application is received after the list of candidates is available, 
then you must send the blank ballot and the list of candidates together, until the regular 
absentee ballot is available.  If the application is received on or after the day regular absentee 
ballots are available, then you should send the regular absentee ballot. 
 
B.  Blank Ballots (For the Election) to Electors Temporarily Living ( or Expecting to Be 
Traveling) Outside United States and to Members of the Armed Forces, Spouses a nd 
Dependents, Whether Living Within or Outside United States 
 
Under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-153f, any elector temporarily living (or expecting to be traveling) 
outside the territorial limits of the United States and any member of the armed forces or the 
spouse or dependent living where such member is stationed, whether living within or outside 
the United States, may request that a blank absentee ballot and complete list of candidates 
and questions be issued to them as soon as such list is available (45 days before the 
election).  This provision applies even if there is no military contingency.  The Official Blank 
Absentee Ballot, together with a complete list of nominated candidates and questions to be 
voted upon at the election, are to be issued to such people beginning as soon as possible 
after a complete list of candidates and questions is available for the primary and for the 
election. The blank ballot must be available to eligible overseas electors no later than 
June 26, 2026, for the August 11, 2026, primary. For the election on November 3, 2026, 
the blank ballot must be available to eligible overseas electors no later than September 
18, 2026. 
 
The list of candidates and questions should state:  "This is not a ballot.  To be counted, all 
votes must be written in on the Official Blank Absentee Ballot (Election) (ED-81a)" and must 
list each office to be voted upon, the number of individuals for which each elector may vote, 
the name of each nominated candidate for each office, each candidate's party designation, 
and a complete list of numbered questions as they will appear on the ballot.  We remind you 
that under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9 -249a, the names of the major parties shall be arranged on 
the ballots in the following Order: 
 
Democratic 
Republican 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
To Municipal Clerks 
Page 3 
 
 
Under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9- 253, when a party  is entitled to nominate more than one  
candidate for an office, the candidates are listed in the order determined by the registrars 
by lot.  Registrars must provide at least five days' public notice of the lottery. 
 
Also, local questions explanatory texts and/or summaries of arguments in favor of, and 
opposed to, local questions may be enclosed with each blank Absentee Ballot  only if the 
legislative body of your municipality has authorized these under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-369b. 
 
The Application for Absentee Ballot (ED -3), has a box clearly indicating this request for an 
early blank ballot.  If the FPCA is used, we advise you to send all electors temporarily living 
outside the United States and all servicemen and their spouses and dependents a blank ballot 
(and a complete list of candidates and questions) as soon as the list is available, because of 
the provisions of §104(f) of the federal "Uniformed and Overseas Citizens Absentee Voting 
Act."  Consequently, people who use the FPCA should be sent the regular printed absentee  
ballot for a regular election only if the FPCA is received by you within 31 days before the 
regular election.  Moreover, in the case of persons who have filed a FPCA in 2026, as soon 
as the complete list of candidates and questions is available, for the municipal election we 
urge you to use Form ED-12 to direct an Official Blank Absentee Ballot and a complete list of 
nominated candidates and questions to an elector or applicant for admission as an elector 
who (1) is temporarily living outside the United States or (2) is a member of the armed forces 
or the spouse or dependent living where the member is stationed.  If the application is received 
on or after the day regular absentee ballots are available, then you should send the regular 
absentee ballot (ED-1).  The federal Help America Vote Act (Sec. 104 of PL 107-252) amends 
UOCAVA and requires states to provide an absentee ballot for only one year from submission 
of the FPCA.  For example, if a voter uses a FPCA in January of 2026  you would honor that 
application in 2026 for all primaries and elections held.   
 
C.  Summary Of Connecticut Blank Ballot Law (For Elections) 
 
In summary, a blank absentee ballot set  for the primary should be sent beginning August 5, 
2026, to people with military contingencies consisting of the following forms: 
  
   ￼Optional Privacy Sleeve 
 ED-11    Return Envelope (postage-free) 
ED-14  
  Clerk to Voter Envelope (postage-free) 
 ED-81a/pr  Blank Ballot 
 ED-10  Statement of List of Offices to be Filled and the Unavailability 
   of List of Candidates and Questions.  (This statement includes the list of 
offices, the number to vote for and a statement that it is not a ballot and that a 
complete list of candidates and questions will be mailed when it becomes 
available.) 
 
Local Question Explanatory Text and/or Summaries of Arguments in favor of, and 
opposed to, local questions (only if the legislative body of your municipality has 
authorized these under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-369b). 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
To Municipal Clerks 
Page 4 
 
 
Beginning as soon as possible after a complete list of candidates and questions is available, the 
above blank absentee ballot set  (without ED-10), together with a complete list of  nominated 
candidates and questions will be sent to (1) all members of the armed forces , spouses and 
dependents who request an early ballot or who submit the FPCA more than 31 days before the 
regular election and who have not already been sent a blank ballot, and (2) all electors 
temporarily living (or expecting to be traveling) outside  the territorial limits of the  United 
States who request an early ballot or who submit the FPCA more than 31 days before the regular 
election. 
 
 
If you have any questions, please do not hesitate to call us at (860) 509-6100. 
 
  
 Sincerely, 
 
 Stephanie Thomas 
 Secretary of the State 
 
  
 By:____________________ 
         Aida Carini 
         Staff Attorney 
 
 
 
Enclosures (ED-10, ED-81a)  
cc:  Registrars of Voters  
 
 
ED-10 (Rev. 6/26 Absentee-Blank Ballot-List-Suggested Form (§9-153e) 
[G\forms\ed-10-99\ed-10]

                                         _____________________________

                *City

                 Borough 
 
 
 
Statement of List of Offices to Be Filled and the 
Unavailability of List of Candidates and Questions 
 
A complete list of the nominated candidates for whom you may vote, with the 
respective party designations of each candidate, and the designation of questions 
is not available at this time, but will be mailed to you as soon as it becomes 
available. (It will probably be mailed during the week of (insert date available for 
primary or election*, 2026.) 
 
A list of offices to be voted upon and the number of individuals for which each

This is Not a Ballot: your ballot is enclosed and entitled "Official Blank Absentee

### LEAD Communication (May 15, 2026) — Circulators and Petitions Frequently Asked Questions

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/may/circulators-and-petitions_faqs_may-15-2026.pdf?rev=f3a1d412d8e04e5ca7266972ec08eb6f&hash=DADE3899BB17A8EB956EEB647F4ACDA6. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Circulators and Petitions Frequently Asked Questions", May 15, 2026.*

Frequently Asked Questions: Primary Petitions and Circulators in Connecticut 
May 2026 
1. Must the primary petition circulator appear in person before a registrar of voters when the registrar fills
out Section C of the petition form?
Answer: No. The circulator need not be present when the registrar fills out Section C. While CGS Sec. 9-404b(d)
requires that each petition page “contain a statement signed by the registrar of the municipality in which the
circulator is an enrolled party member attesting that the circulator is an enrolled party member in the
municipality,” it does not require that the circulator be present when the registrar makes that 
attestation. Generally, the registrar should be able to make that determination by checking CVRS.  If the
registrar does need more information, for example, a father and son with the same name living at the same
address, the registrar may contact the would-be circulator.  However, they may not mandate that the circulator
appear in person to complete Section C.
2. May a circulator make copies of the circulator form, signed by a registrar, or must each individual form
be signed by the registrar?
Answer: No, a circulator may not make copies of the form. Each form must contain the original signature of the
applicable registrar of voters.
3. Is someone who has not voted in the last four elections prohibited from signing a petition?
Answer: No. If a person is on the party’s enrollment list—whether active or inactive—they may sign a
petition. If an inactive voter signs a petition, the registrar must put them on the active list if the address
listed on the petition matches their registration address. (CGS 9-35c)
4. When should a registrar sign Section C of the petition form?  Before or after signatures are collected?
Answer: A registrar must sign Section C before the petitions are turned in.  A registrar is not required to
sign the form before signatures are collected, but it is recommended that they do so because if it is
later found that the circulator is not a party member, then any signatures collected by that circulator
will not be counted.
5. Is written authorization from the candidate required for a registrar to sign Section C of the petition
form?
Answer: No
6. May a circulator fill out the petition information for a signer, other than the person’s signature?
Answer: No, assistance may not be provided unless the person wishing to sign the petition asks for 
assistance. The circulator may not fill out the form ahead of time.
7. Must signatures from electors who have not been registered in a party for three months before signing 
a petition be rejected?
Answer: It depends on the date that party privileges attach.  For example, a switch from one party to 
another party creates a three-month waiting period before party privileges attach. In this situation, the 
signature would be invalid. However, if an individual is unaffiliated and changes their registration to a 
party, this switch does not create a waiting period.  In this situation, the signature would be valid. (CGS 
Sec. 9-59)
8. Must petition sheets submitted by a petition circulator who has not been registered in a party for three 
months before having the registrar sign off in Section C of the petition sheet be rejected?
Answer: As above, it depends on the date that party privileges attach.  If an individual, who is 
registered with one party changes their registration to another party, this switch creates a three-month 
waiting period before party privileges attach. In this situation, the circulator would not be eligible to 
circulate primary petitions, and any signatures that were collected by them would be invalid. At the 
same time, an individual who is unaffiliated may register with a party, and this switch does not create a 
waiting period.  In this situation, the circulator would be eligible, and all the signatures that they 
collected would be valid.  (CGS Sec. 9-59)

From the Office of the Secretary of the State  
165 Capitol Avenue , Hartford , CT 06106 
 
                                   March 23, 2026 
To all Town Clerks : 
 
Re:            Selection of Delegates  
 
We are enclosing the required forms in connection with the selection of delegates to conventions 
under Sections 9-390, 9-391, 9-393, and 9-395 of the General Statutes.  
 
Selection of Delegates  
 
Delegates to conventions must be selected, in accordance with the rules of the party, by one of two 
methods prescribed by Connecticut General Statutes Section 9 -390.  The two methods are: (1) 
Selection made by party enrolled members of such party in such municipality in a caucus or (2) 
Selection made by the town committee of such party.   
 
Notice of Caucus 
 
If a caucus is the endorsing authority of delegates, the town chairman or their designee must give 
notice in a newspaper having general circulation in the town of the date, time, location and purpose 
of such caucus held.  The notice must be given not less than five full days before the date set for the 
caucus; provided, if the rules of the party in any municipality require earlier notice, the party rules 
shall prevail.  P lease refer to the November 3, 2026, State Election Calendar , which is available here.  
C.G.S. §9-390 
 
To reduce the likelihood of inadvertent failures-to-endorse, you may wish to remind the party officials 
in your town that (a) all delegate selections must be made only between March 24 and March 31, 
2026, and (b) selections must be certified to the Town Clerk by April 1, 2026 .  The failure to file a 
timely certification nullifies an otherwise timely selection, and such a situation is not a "vacancy ," 
which may be filled at a later date. 
 
Selection of Delegates from Political Subdivision  
 
If delegates are to be selected from a political subdivision (i.e. part of a town), only the enrolled party 
members or town committee members, as the case may be, from such political subdivision may 
participate, except that (1) in a municipality in which t he town committee is elected at -large and is 
the selecting authority, the town committee as a whole shall select, and (2) in a municipality in which 
the town committee is elected from political subdivisions and is the selecting authority, whenever no 
member of the town committee resides in such political subdivision or district from which the 
selection is to be made, the town committee as a whole shall select.  

 
   
 
 
Selection of Delegates Deemed Lawful  
 
If delegates are selected in accordance with the provisions of party rules and by one of the methods 
discussed above, the delegates shall be deemed to have been lawfully selected.  There is no longer 
an opportunity to primary for delegate selection.  
 
Notification of Selection of Delegates  
 
The selection of delegates must be certified to the clerk of the municipality by the chairman or 
presiding officer or the secretary of the town committee or caucus not later than 4:00 pm . on April 
1, 2026.  Each such certific ate shall contain the name and street address of each person selected, the 
position as delegate, and the name or number of the political subdivision or district for which each 
such person is selected.  If such a certificate of a party’s selection is not received by t he clerk of the 
municipality by this dead line, such party shall be deemed to have neither made nor certified any 
selection of any person for the position of delegate.  Please Note:  The State Central Committees of 
each political party will provide this form  to the town chairs.   
 
Vacancies in Delegation  
 
The rules of a party may provide methods for the filling of vacancies in delegations to the convention, 
which methods may include prescribing that each delegate selected in conformity with the 
Connecticut General Statutes may designate an alternate delegate or a proxy to act for such delegate 
in their absence.  
 
A.  Notices of Delegate Selection s  
 
We are enclosing two forms for use during the delegate selection : (1) the notice of certification of 
party-selected delegates and (2) the notice for failure to receive certification of party -selected 
delegates.  T he Town Clerk is required to publish the notice under Section 9 -395.  The Secretary of 
the State is required to prescribe the form of the notice.  One copy of each of the prescribed forms 
of Notice of Selected Delegates (ED -616), and Failure to Select (ED -617) is enclosed.  (The Town Clerk 
must forthwith publish any change in the party -selected candidates listing such changes.)  
 
If you have any questions in connection with these forms, or if we can be of service to you in any way, 
please do not hesitate to get in touch with Lewis Button at (860) 509-6100. 
  
Sincerely, 
Stephanie Thomas 
Secretary of the State  
Enclosures (ED-616 and ED-617)   
 
Information copies to:  
Registrars of Voters (letter only)  
Republican and Democratic State Central Committees

Selection of Delegates  
 
Delegates to conventions must be selected, in accordance with the rules of the party, by one of two

### LEAD Communication (June 5, 2026) — Detailed Curing Instructions

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/june/detailed-curing-instructions.pdf?rev=9960fb8c8eb3432b87f7b9ae4457e31f&hash=8E57C25D81F339E66D5ADC08099EDF93. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Detailed Curing Instructions", June 5, 2026.*

Guidelines for Curing Absentee Ballots Submitted with  
Outdated Inner Envelopes 
General Requirement- Staffing: 
For curing purposes, two (2) Town Clerk staff members must be present when opening 
the outer envelope. 
1. Opening the Outer Envelope
Do not immediately remove all contents.
After breaking the seal on the outer envelope:
1. Partially open the flap and visually inspect the contents.
2. Determine whether:
o An inner envelope is present, and
o Whether the ballot is inside the inner envelope or loose in the outer
envelope.
Proceed according to the scenarios below: 
2. Ballot is inside an inner envelope
A. Inner envelope is signed
• Remove the inner envelope.
• Verify that the voter signed the inner envelope.
• If signed: Reseal the outer envelope with tape and process the ballot normally.
B. Inner envelope is not signed
• Remove the inner envelope, keeping the ballot sealed inside.
• Because the inner envelope lacks a signature, initiate the curing process.*
3. Ballot is not inside the inner envelope
A. Inner envelope is present, but the ballot was not inserted into it.
• Remove only the inner envelope. Do not touch or remove the ballot.
• Check if the inner envelope is signed.
o If the inner envelope is signed, process the ballot normally.
o If the inner envelope is not signed, initiate the curing process.*
B. The inner envelope was not inserted into the outer envelope
• If the outer envelope contains only the absentee ballot with no inner envelope,
the ballot must be rejected. No curing allowed under these
circumstances.**
4. Multiple ballots in the outer envelope
A. Two ballots, no inner envelope
• If the outer envelope contains two ballots and no inner envelope, both 
ballots must be rejected.**
B. One ballot inside the inner envelope and one ballot loose
• If one ballot is properly enclosed in the inner envelope and one ballot is 
loose:
→ Reject the ballot that is outside the inner envelope.
 Because the absentee ballot does not include an outer envelope, it 
must be placed and sealed inside a manila envelope.
 On the front of the manila envelope, clearly write:
Rejected: No Inner Envelope
 No updates shall be made in CVRS
 Process the ballot in the inner envelope normally if the inner 
envelope is signed.
C. Two inner envelopes enclosed
• If two inner envelopes are present:
 Process the inner envelope corresponding to the issued outer 
envelope normally, if it is signed.
 The inner envelope not corresponding to the outer envelope (i.e., 
not the correct voter) must be rejected.
 Because the second absentee ballot does not include an outer 
envelope, it must be placed and sealed inside a manila envelope.
 On the front of the manila envelope, clearly write:
Rejected: Two Ballots in Serial Numbered Envelope
 No updates shall be made to CVRS
* Curing Instructions
1. Any absentee ballot awaiting curing  must be placed inside a larger,
sealed manila envelope.
2. On the front of the manila envelope, please include the following
information:
• The serial number of the envelope
• The voter’s name
• The voter’s voting address
• The district
• The date of the election, primary, or referendum
3. (You may attach the absentee ballot label generated in CVRS
instead of writing this information manually.)
4. Below the voter informat ion, the following statement must appear:
Cure Eligible: Missing Signature
5. The manila envelope may only be opened by the voter .
6. The manila envelope must also be segregated from all properly
received absentee ballots and securely stored in the Town
Clerk’s vault.
7. The voter’s absentee ballot record in CVRS must be updated.
>Under Return Type, select: “Cure Eligible.”
**Rejected Ballots 
 Any absentee ballot that is rejected must be sealed with tape to 
prevent further handling.
 On the serial-numbered envelope, the Town Clerk must clearly 
write:  Rejected: Missing Signature
 The Town Clerk must also update the voter’s absentee ballot record
in CVRS.
>Under Return Type, select: “Rejected.
3. The rejected ballot must be segregated from all properly received
absentee ballots and must not be turned over for counting
purposes.

ED-606a [Rev. 02/20, g/forms/ED-600's/ed-606a.doc] Head Mod. Return, Mun. or T.Comm. Prim. 
 
State of Connecticut 
Office of the Secretary of the State 
Election Services Division 
 
 
Prescribed Form For Return Of Votes Cast At A Municipal Or 
Town Committee Primary 
 (C.G.S. §9-440 and . §9-314(b)) 
 
The head moderator shall file a preliminary list of the vote totals produced by the tabulators not later than midnight on 
election day.  Thereafter, the head moderator shall file a complete head moderator’s return with the Secretary of the State 
by electronic means not later than forty -eight hours after the close of the polls.  The head moderator shall also seal and 
deliver an original head moderator’s return to the Secretary of the State not later than the third day after the election. 
 
The head moderator in each town shall deliver to town clerk one copy of duplicate certificate of votes cast for candidates 
not later than forty-eight hours after the close of the polls.  Use additional pages, if necessary.
