(ED-619 - Rev. 05/26 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. At-Large / Political Subdivision)  
   
 
Instruction Page for Primary Petition Page for Primary Petition 
for Municipal Office(s) At-Large / Political Subdivision 
Note: (1) Complete on this Instruction Page, in the space provided, the total number of 
signatures needed for a candidacy for the nomination to the particular state office(s); (2) Provide 
at least one instruction page for each circulator; (3) Provide one or more  Primary Petition 
Signatures Pages suitable for  duplication as you deem necessary (however, if the  candidate or 
candidates are indigent, provide at least two times the number of signature pages needed to 
contain the required number of signatures); and (4) Complete all blanks in Part A of each primary 
petition signature page provided. 
Request for Petition Forms: Any person who requests a petition form must give to the 
Registrar of Voters before they may obtain the form (1) their own name and address, and the 
name, address, and office sought of each candidate for whom the petition is being obtained; 
and (2) a statement signed by each such candidate that they consent to be a candidate for 
said office. Each candidate must include their name as they authorize it to appear on the 
ballot on their statement of consent. After a person has completed the foregoing 
requirements, the Registrar, before giving them the petition form, must fill in on each Petition 
Signatures Page the name and address of each candidate to be named therein, the office 
sought by each such candidate, the name of the political party holding the primary, and the 
date of the primary, and must also fill in the blanks on this Instruction Page. 
 
Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be Provided:  Upon completion of the above 
requirements, the Registrar  shall give to the person requesting the petition form one or more 
signature pages suitable for duplication as he deems necessary. If the candidate or candidates 
are indigent, the Registrar shall provide a number of pages determined by the Registrar  as at 
least two times the number sufficient to contain the required number of signatures. The number 
of signatures required is at least 5% of the electors whose names appear on the active last- 
completed enrollment list of the party in the municipality or such lesser number as the party 
rules prescribe. The number of signatures required is at least _____________. 
 
Time of Filing : This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party whose 
nomination is sought and in the town in which such petition was circulated not later than 4:00 
p.m. on June 09, 2026. 
General Information: A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a party- 
endorsed candidate) whose name appears on the last- completed enrollment list of the party 
within any municipality in the State by filing the required petition with the registrar of the party. 
A petition may propose as many candidates for different offices as there are candidates to be 
nominated by the party statewide, but no more.  If duplicated, petition pages should be 
duplicated front to back exactly as they appear in this packet. 
 
Misrepresentation Prohibited : It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally 
misrepresent the contents of a primary petition. A Class D felony is punishable by imprisonment 
for a term of not less than one year nor more than five years, or a fine not exceeding $5,000, 
or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature of the 
signer; no one, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, may sign for another. Any 
person who signs a name other than their  own to a primary petition or who  signs a 
name other than their own as a circulator of such a petition shall be fined not  more 
than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one year or both. Signatures shall 
be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page distributed by the Registrar of Voters or 
on duplicates of the pages issued by the Registrar . No person shall sign as a petition signer 
more than once for the same candidate or candidates. Petition signatures may not be withdrawn. 
Only the signatures of electors whose names appear upon the last -completed enrollment list of 
the party holding the primary in the municipality will be counted. If the registrar determines that 
an enrolled elector on the inactive registry list signed a petition giving the same address as on 
the inactive registry list, such name shall forthwith be placed on the active registry list and 
counted. Signers who have moved within town should be encouraged to give their birth date 
because the registrar is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the street address on the 
petition is different from the street address on the enrollment list if (1) the signer is eligible to 
vote for the candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown on the  petition is 
the same as the birth date on the signer's registration record. 
(ED-619 - Rev. 05/26 Instruction Page for Prim. Ptn. At-Large / Political Subdivision)  
   
 
Assistance to People who are Blind in the Signing of Petitions : Any person who is blind 
may cause their name to be placed on a petition if an authorized agent (not the circulator) reads 
aloud the full text of the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the blind person consents 
to having their name appear on it.  If a blind person is unable to write, their authorized agent 
may write the name of the blind person, followed by the word "by" and the agent’s own signature. 
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party holding the primary and a 
United States citizen at least 18 years of age.  No person may circulate petitions for more than 
the maximum number of candidates to be nominated by a party for the same office.  No 
candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition for another 
candidate (or another group of candidates contained in one petition) for the nomination of the 
party for the same office. A candidate may circulate a primary petition in which he is proposed 
as a candidate. Any petition page circulated in violation of these provisions of the law must be 
rejected by the registrar. The circulator must be certain to have signers sign in their presence, 
and that the circulator’s own signed statements are true, or the circulator s hall be guilty of a 
Class A misdemeanor, and the  whole page may be rejected.  A Class A misdemeanor is 
punishable by up to 364 days in prison, a fine of up to $2,000, or both. 
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member:  
Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the appropriate 
party attesting that the circulator has represented that they are an enrolled member of such 
party. This statement may be completed at any time, but must be completed before the petition 
page is submitted for filing to the party's registrar in the municipality of the voting residences of 
the signers. Unless such a statement by t he registrar appears on each page so submitted, the 
page must be rejected by the registrar. 
 
Circulator's Statement of  Authenticity of Signatures : The Circulator's Statement of  
Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be 
completed and signed by the circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing the 
page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters. The page must be rejected by the registrar if the 
Statement is not completed and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer : After signing the Circulator's Statement of 
Authenticity of Signatures, the circulator must appear before one of the following officers to 
acknowledge the statement: a judge of a court of record, clerk or deputy clerk of a court having 
a seal, family support magistrate, town clerk, notary public, justice of the peace, or attorney 
admitted to the Connecticut Bar. The acknowledgment must be completed and signed by the 
officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the registrar if this is not 
done. 
 
"Registrar" Defined: "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political party 
holding a primary and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for different 
voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at the last -
preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of the vote 
of the whole municipality was the moderator. 
 
Offices: 
This primary petition form is to be used in connection with the following municipal offices when 
applicable: 
 
1. A town, city, or borough office for which all electors of such town, city, or borough 
may vote; 
2. A town, city, or borough office for which only the electors of a political subdivision 
of such town, city, or borough may vote 
3. State Representative in an Assembly District composed of a single town; 
4. State Representative in an Assembly District composed of a part of a single town; 
5. Judge of Probate in a Probate District composed of a single town, and;  
6. Registrar of Voters, whether elected from a voting district or voting districts. 
(ED-619 - Rev. 4/24) 
 
   
 
Warning: It is a crime to sign this petition in the name of another person without 
legal authority to do so, and you may not sign this petition if you are not an elector.

### LEAD Communication (June 2026) — Absentee Curing Frequently Asked Questions

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/june/faq-absentee-curing---pa-26-42.pdf?rev=714f3dd9e3be4ddd99cdd329598af843&hash=54D7EFC22E3595C1D278F057DBEF7CD2. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Absentee Curing Frequently Asked Questions", June 2026.*

FAQs on Changes AﬀecƟng Absentee VoƟng 
Public Act 26-42 (HB 5001) 
Handling Pre-ExisƟng Materials 
1. Can voters use and town clerks accept the old envelopes? In that case, may town clerks 
open the envelopes and check them for a signature before elecƟon day?  
Answer: Town Clerks should make every eﬀort to acquire the new envelopes from LEAD and 
distribute them going forward. (Certainly, starƟng with the August 2026 primary, the new 
materials will be in circulaƟon and must be used.) To the extent a voter used an absentee 
voƟng set before the passage of PA 26-42, the envelope and ballots must be accepted.  
2. How should towns process AB applicaƟons that were submiƩed under the prior “reason 
required” system now that the new law is eﬀecƟve? Are previously submiƩed applicaƟons 
sƟll valid without requiring new applicaƟons? 
 
Answer: Town clerks should honor Absentee Ballot ApplicaƟons submiƩed under the prior, 
“reason required” system. This includes applicaƟons in which an applicant failed to check oﬀ 
a reason.   
 
3. If ABs have already been issued using the prior inner envelope (signature required, no cure 
process), but new ABs must be issued using the updated cure-eligible envelope, how should 
clerks handle both types of envelopes in circulaƟon simultaneously? 
Answer: Town clerks must provide an opportunity to cure any returned absentee ballot 
missing a signature.  This means the clerk must allow a voter who received and returns an 
old AB set to cure a missing signature on the inner envelope. 
4. Ballots issued aŌer the law went into eﬀect may be cured, while those issued before that 
Ɵme did not oﬀer the opportunity for a cure. Is this discrepancy permissible? Are clerks 
required or permiƩed to reissue materials to ensure uniformity? 
 
Answer: Town clerks should avoid issuing new ballots unless speciﬁcally requested by a 
voter.  As stated above, town clerks should provide all AB voters who qualify with the 
opportunity to cure.   
 
5. How should municipal clerks eﬀectuate curing with ballots returned in the old envelopes? 
 
 
2 
 
Answer: The municipal clerk should open the outer envelope of returned ABs to check the 
inner envelope for a signature. If the inner envelope is signed, keep both envelopes together 
and sort them as required. There is nothing further to be done here. If the inner envelope is 
not signed, keep the envelopes together and do not sort them with the other, sorted 
ballots. These ballots require curing. The clerk must use their best eﬀorts to contact the 
impacted voters (see below). We advise that this procedure be performed with two people, 
preferably staﬀ members of the Town Clerk’s oﬃce.  Importantly, if there is no inner 
envelope, take special care to maintain voter privacy; do not look at the ballot. 
(See aƩached Curing InstrucƟons for further details.) 
  
Curing Process 
 
6. When does the curing process start and end?  
 
Answer: Under the new law, voters may cure their ballots from the beginning of the 
absentee voƟng period unƟl the close of the polls on the day of the elecƟon.   
 
7. What must town clerks do if a voter provided no contact informaƟon other than a mailing 
address?  
 
Answer: If a voter provides no contact informaƟon other than a mailing address, the clerk 
must exercise discreƟon in contacƟng the voter through other means.  At the very least, we 
recommend sending a leƩer through USPS, as soon as possible. We will provide a cure 
noƟﬁcaƟon leƩer template. 
 
8. What must town clerks do if they reach a voter, but the voter is unable to come into the 
oﬃce to cure the ballot?   
 
Answer:  The law only requires eﬀorts to noƟfy the voter. The clerk can inform the voter 
that failure to cure the ballot will result in the ballot being rejected. The voter may 
parƟcipate in early voƟng or in-person voƟng on elecƟon day instead of curing the absentee 
ballot. In this case, the registrar may inform the town clerk when the voter is parƟcipaƟng in 
early or elecƟon day voƟng. Then, the clerk shall reject the absentee ballot. If the registrar 
does not noƟfy the clerk, the clerk must reject the ballot as it was not cured by the close of 
polls on elecƟon day. 
 
9. May the town clerk send a new absentee ballot instead of having the voter appear before 
the clerk or vote at polls or during early voƟng?  
 
Answer: No (see previous 8. QuesƟon and Response) 
 
 
3 
 
10. Is there a speciﬁc Ɵme absentee ballots must be delivered to the central count locaƟon, and 
should town clerks hold back the envelopes that are eligible for curing?  
 
Answer: Absentee ballots can be cured at the municipal clerk’s oﬃce unƟl the polls close on 
elecƟon day. No curing is allowed aŌer the polls close.  Stored absentee ballots are to be 
delivered by 8:00 a.m. on elecƟon day. 
 
11. What is considered best eﬀort when contacƟng a voter?  
Answer: Under the new law, “best eﬀorts” include calling the elector and emailing them if a 
telephone number or email was provided on either their voter registraƟon or their 
applicaƟon for an absentee ballot. If no phone number or email was provided, clerks must 
use their discreƟon to contact the voter. The clerk must use best eﬀorts to contact impacted 
voters within 24-hours aŌer receiving the ballot. Beginning 24 hours before the close of 
polls, the clerk must aƩempt to contact these voters immediately. 
12. Is there a requirement for how many attempts to contact a voter for curing? 
Answer: The law does not specify a number of attempts. It requires town clerks to use their 
best efforts and exercise discretion. We suggest that town clerks maintain a log of their 
contact efforts including method, date, and time.  
13. Is there a requirement to log/journal attempts? 
Answer: There is no legal requirement to maintain a record of contact attempts. However, 
we advise that clerks maintain a record for their own protection. 
14. Could the “notes” field in CVRS be used to log the attempts? 
Answer: Currently, CVRS does not have this functionality. 
Town Clerk Duties 
15. Must town clerks be in their oﬃces on ElecƟon Day? 
Answer: The municipal clerk must be available to cure absentee ballots unƟl the polls close 
on elecƟon day.

Office of the Secretary of the State 
State of Connecticut 
165 Capitol Avenue Hartford, CT 06106 
 
 May 21, 2026 
 
To All Town Clerks: 
 
Re:  Two (2) Form Notices in Connection with the Selection of Candidates for 
            “Municipal Offices” to be filled at the November 3, 2026, State Election      
 
Notices 
 
We are enclosing two forms of notice in connection with the nomination of major party candidates for 
"municipal office" under the state primary law. 
 
These forms are being provided to you in connection with the nominations for the municipal offices of State 
Representative in an assembly district composed of a single town or a multi-town; Registrar of Voters; and 
other town offices, for those few town s which elect some town officials at a state election under a special 
act.  (The enclosed notices are not used for senatoria l and assembly districts that cross town lines; for 
example, they are not used for a district composed of parts of two towns.) 
 
 1.  The first form enclosed is Form ED-619b,  entitled "Party Endorsed Candidates for Municipal 
Offices", which the Town Clerk is required to publish under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-395.  The Secretary of the 
State is required to prescribe the form of such notice, and the enclosed is our prescribed form. 
 
The law requires the Town Clerk to publish this notice not later than the seventy-sixth (76
th) day preceding 
the day of the primary, May 27th.  Under Conn. Gen.  Stat. §9-391, subsection (a), party endorsements for 
all municipal offices must be made between May 19th an d May 26th of this year.  Such endorsements for 
the municipal offices or city offices elected by special act must be certified to the town clerk no later 
than 4:00 p.m. on the 14 th day after the close of the town comm ittee meeting, caucus, or convention 
which made the endorsement.  However, endorsements for the municipal office of State Representative 
and Registrar of Voters will now be certified to the Office of the Secretary of the State  not later than 
4:00 p.m. on the 14 th day after the close of th e town committee meeting, caucus, or convention that 
made the endorsement.  Therefore,  the deadline for a party's certification of its endorsement of a state 
representative from a single-town district will be between June 2nd and June 9th , depending on which 
day the endorsement was made during the period between May 19th and May 26th.  Our office will notify 
each municipal clerk of any party endorsements filed for the office of single town state representative and 
registrar of voters as soon as possible after such notices have been filed with us. 
 
Certification of endorsements for the municipal office of State Representative will be on a form prepared 
by this office and provided to the state central comm ittees of the two major parties.  The certification of 
endorsements for the other municipal offices should be on a form provided by our office (but may not be).  
The certification will be signed by the endorsed candida te, and it will indicate how their name will appear 
on the ballot.  Finally, the certification must be certified by the chairman, presiding officer, or the secretary 
of the endorsing body. 
 
 
 
  
May 20, 2026 
Page 2 
 
 
 
 2.   A Notice for "Failure to Receive Certificati on of Party-Endorsed Candidates for Municipal Offices" 
is no longer required to be published by state statute.   
 
In order to reduce the likelihood of inadvertent fa ilure-to-endorse, you may wish to remind the party 
officials in your town of (a) the certification deadlin es outlined above, and (b) the fact that all municipal 
endorsements must be made between May 19th and Ma y 26th, inclusive - i.e., if an endorsement for a 
particular office is not made at that time, this does not produce a "vacancy" which may be filled by a later 
endorsement under party rules.  (The only vacancies in endorsement, which may be so filled, are those 
resulting from the withdrawal or death of a candidate originally endorsed at the proper time.)  Moreover, 
the failure to file a timely certification nullifies an otherwise timely endorsement. 
 
You may also wish to remind the party officials in y our town that if the caucus is the endorsing authority 
for municipal office, Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-390 requir es the town chairman or their designee to give 
newspaper notice of the date, time, location, and purpose of the caucus at least five days before the caucus, 
unless party rules require earlier notice. 
 
 3.  The second form enclosed is Form ED-619e, en titled "Notice of Primary."  The Town Clerk must 
publish this notice under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-435 when a primary for a municipal office is to be held. 
Although we are not required to prescribe this form, we have prepared this suggested form for your 
convenience.  This form also incorporates the notice that the Registrar of Voters must give to you under the 
same statute; therefore, a copy of this notice is bei ng included with the informational copy of this letter 
being sent to all Registrars of voters. 
 
 
If you have any questions, please call us at (860) 509-6100. 
 
  Sincerely, 
 
  Stephanie Thomas 
  Secretary of the State 
 
 
  By:____________________ 
            Bernard Liu    
            Staff Attorney 
 
 
 
Enclosures:  ED-6l9b, ED-619e

Section IV: Storage Plan  
Please note: If you are using an alternative store plan that does not follow the chain of custody 
procedure prescribed by the Early Voting Handbook and outlined below, please move to Section V. 
At the conclusion of each day during the early voting period, registrars of voters must transport the 
receptacles containing the day’s early voting ballots (and SDR ballots in the case of a general election) to 
the municipal clerk. The clerk must secure and store them in a manner as similar as possible to the 
security and storage of absentee ballots. The election oﬃcials who are present when the registrars

transport the ballots to the town clerk must sign the Aﬃdavit of Delivery and Receipt of Early Voting 
Ballots: Towns Where Town Clerks Store Ballots.

conﬁrmed, etc. 
Section V: Alternative Storage Plan 
Registrars operating under an alternative storage plan must submit it to the Secretary of the State for 
approval. The plan should indicate the basis for the request and set out a clear chain of custody and 
storage procedures. At a minimum, the registrars must store the ballots under lock and key in a tamper-
proof, ﬁre-proof receptacle. The receptacle must be locked in a secure room that is not generally 
accessible. When delivering ballots for counting on the day of the primary or election, these towns will 
use the Aﬃdavit of Delivery and Receipt of Early Voting Ballots: Towns Operating Under an Alternative 
Storage Plan

(List each staﬀ member and their position, as well as each conﬁrmed alternate and their position)

Section VI: Early Voting Location on Certain College Campuses 
Please note: By law, registrars of voters must designate an additional early voting location on a campus 
of a constituent unit of higher education in a municipality if at least 1,000 students live on campus or in 
institution-owned, -operated, or -aﬃliated housing.  Constituent units are (1) UConn and its campuses 
and (2) the Connecticut State Colleges and Universities.

Section VII: Optional Additional Location(s) in Towns with a Population of 20,000 
or More 
Please note: By law, the local legislative body must determine whether to have an optional additional 
location or locations, even if the town will not have such a location.

### LEAD Communication (January 6, 2026) — Town Committee Notices - Town Clerks

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/january/town-committee-notices_town-clerks_01-06-25.pdf?rev=0cf3db0590f14d4eaf1b75f37beab51d&hash=DB4FF02E85B21A9FE758A81ABF4F7CAF. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Town Committee Notices - Town Clerks", January 6, 2026.*

Secretary of the State  
165 Capitol Ave 
Hartford, CT 06106 
 To All Town Clerks: January 6, 2026 
 We are enclosing five forms of Notice in connection with the election of Town Committee members under the State Primary Law. 
Two of these are the forms of Notice of Party-Endorsed Candidates, which the municipal clerk is required to publish under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-395. The Secretary of the State is required to prescribe the form of such notice, and one copy of each of our prescribed forms for Town Committee At-Large (ED-611) and Town Committee from Political Subdivisions (ED-6lla) is enclosed. 
We have also enclosed Failure to Endorse forms (ED-613 and ED-613a), which the municipal clerk must publish when no endorsements are made. In addition to the normal failure to endorse situation, this will also occur in those towns whose parties have adopted the direct primary method of election. 
The fifth form of notice is the Notice of Primary (ED-612), which the municipal clerk must publish under §9-435. Although we are not required to prescribe this form, we have prepared a suggested form for your convenience. This form also incorporates the notice which the registrar of voters must give to you under said §9-435. 
To reduce the likelihood of inadvertent failures-to-endorse, you may wish to remind the party officials in your town that (a) all party endorsements may be made only between January 6 and January 13, 2026, and (b) endorsements must be certified to you by 4:00 p.m. on January 14, 2026. If an endorsement is not made between these dates, this does not produce a "vacancy" which may be filled by a later endorsement under party rules. (The only vacancies in endorsement which may be so filled are those resulting from the withdrawal or death of a candidate originally endorsed at the proper time.) Moreover, the failure to file a timely certification nullifies an otherwise timely endorsement. 
You may also wish to remind the town chairmen that §9-390 requires the town chairman or his designee to give newspaper notice of date, time, location, and purpose of a caucus at least five full days prior to the caucus, unless party rules require earlier notice. 
 Please call us at (860) 509-6100 whenever this office can be of service to you. 
 Sincerely, 
 
LEAD 
 
 
 
 Enclosures: ED-611, ED-611a, ED-613, ED-613a, ED-612 
Informational copies to: Registrars of Voters

### LEAD Communication (January 7, 2026) — Town Committee - Primary Petitions

*Source: https://portal.ct.gov/-/media/sots/electionservices/lead_communications/2026/january/town-committee_primary-petitions_01-06-26.pdf?rev=4affd87ceee54b58b45c8b0b352ddcff&hash=D3682CBA8FFB2A9CDBEECEDE78679E4F. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: Index heading "2026 LEAD Communications (Legislation and Elections Administration Division)"; entries run January 6, 2026 through at least August 2026 (latest observed items dated 08/10/2026). The hub page lists 2026 and 2023 without the archived flag and marks 2017-2022 plus 'Archived LEAD Communications' with `?archived=true`. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Listed on the authority's 2026 LEAD Communications index as "Town Committee - Primary Petitions", January 7, 2026.*

SECRETARY OF THE STATE 165 Capitol Ave 
HARTFORD, CT 06106 
 
January 6, 2026 
 
To All Registrars of Voters: 
 
Re: Primary Petition Forms: Town Committee-At-Large; 
Town Committee from Political Subdivision 
 
 
In accordance with Conn. Gen Stat. §9-410, we are enclosing two primary petition signature pages 
and instruction pages, which are prescribed for use in connection with candidacies for election to 
town committee. If a party in your municipality by rule provides for the election of members of 
its town committee from the municipality at large, the form of petition that must be used is that 
for Town Committee At-Large (ED-610). If such a party provides for the election of members of 
its town committee from political subdivisions of your municipality, then the proper petition form 
is that for Town Committee from Political Subdivisions (ED-610a). 
 
We urge that you keep the enclosed forms for your file. They are the official forms prescribed 
under §9-410, and they should not be altered in any way when preparing and providing primary 
petition forms for the town committee. When such pages are prepared by you and duplicated, they 
should be produced front to back so that the signature page and the corresponding circulator's 
certificate both appear on the same piece of paper. 
 
Pursuant to §9-409, petition forms shall be available from the registrar "beginning on the day 
following the making of the party's endorsement of a candidate or candidates for such office or 
position, or beginning on the day following the final day for the making of such endorsement under 
the provisions of §9-391, whichever comes first." (January 6 through January 13, 2026, are the 
endorsement dates; January 14th is the day following the final day for making of endorsements). 
If a party does not hold an endorsement caucus (i.e., has a "direct primary" under party rules), or 
fails to make any endorsements during the January 6th - 13th time period, January 14th is the first 
day that petitions may be issued in such cases. 
 
Please remember to complete the blank on each instruction page, relating to the number of 
signatures required, and Part A of each primary petition signature page, prior to issuing the same. 
The deadline for the filing of primary petitions is 4:00 p.m. on January 28, 2026. Please 
remember that all registrars of voters' offices must be open between 1:00 p.m. and 4:00 p.m. 
on January 28, 2026. (§9-405) 
 
Section 9-412 provides that upon receipt of the petition pages, the registrar must give to the person 
who submitted them a signed receipt stating the number of pages and the date and time of filing. 
It also provides that after certifying on each page the number of signers who are enrolled electors, 
the registrar must file the pages with the town clerk, together with his certificate as to the whole 
number of names on the last-completed enrollment list of such party in such town or political 
subdivision, as the case may be. 
 
Please remind Town Chairmen that they must give newspaper notice of caucuses for Town 
Committee endorsements at least five full days in advance thereof (or earlier if so required by 
local party rules). If a caucus is to be held on January 6th, 2026, for example, the notice must be 
published not later than January 1, 2026. The notice must specify the date, time, location, and 
purpose of the caucus. (§9-390, subsection (a)) 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 
 
To All Registrars of Voters  
January 6, 2026 
Page2 
 
 
Active And Inactive Enrollment List 
 
In accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §§9-35, 9-54, 9-55, 9-64a, 9-64b, and 9-396, there is an active 
and inactive enrollment list for each party. The inactive list consists of persons who failed to 
respond to the Notice of Confirmation of Voting Residence (ED-642). We remind you that under 
Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-35, in 2012 Notices of Confirmation of Voting Residence may only be sent out 
between January 1st and May 1st, except that under Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-23g(c), if a Notice of 
Acceptance of a mail-in application card is returned undelivered, a Notice of Confirmation of 
Voting Residence is sent out throughout the year. 
 
We strongly recommend that in accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-42 and §9-42-1 of Conn. 
State Regs., a registrar, deputy registrar or assistant registrar of voters attend each caucus with 
an active and inactive enrollment list and voter registration applications for the purpose of restoring 
persons from the inactive to the active enrollment list before they vote in the caucus. (They may 
also bring transfer of address forms ED-513a under §9-35). After the caucuses, if any, the registrar, 
deputy or assistant registrar of voters should file in your office all restorations signed by the 
enrolled electors. (Only one official is required to restore except on election day.) We remind you, 
however, that under Conn. Gen. Stat. §§9-56 and 9-57, persons who file an application for 
enrollment on the day of a caucus do not acquire enrollment privileges until after the caucus. 
 
In accordance with Conn. Gen. Stat. §9-35c and §9-406 the number of signatures required on a 
primary petition is 5% of the number of enrolled electors on the active enrollment list last printed 
under §9-55, excluding the names of all individuals who have ceased to be electors. When 
checking signatures on primary petitions, however, the signatures of all persons who have 
enrollment privileges in the party shall be counted and, under §9-35c, if an elector on the inactive 
enrollment list signs a petition giving the same address as appears on the inactive list, forthwith 
place such name on the active list and count the signature (no restoration form is necessary in such 
case). 
 
If you have any questions or if we can be of service in any way, please do not hesitate to get in 
touch with your dedicated Elections Officer. 
 
Best, 
 
LEAD 
 
 
Enclosures: 
ED-610, ED-610a 
Informational copies to: 
Town Clerks 
Republican and Democratic State Central Committees 
(ED-610 Rev.12/03 - Under Secs. 9-6b, 9-368c, 9-372(12), 9-405 through 9-412, 9-421, and 9-426 [1] 
(g\forms\ed-600's\ed-610.doc) 
 
 
Instruction Page for Primary Petition for Town Committee At-Large 
 
Note to Registrars: (1) Complete on this Instruction Page, in the space provided, the total number of signatures needed for a candidacy for election to the town committee; (2) provide at least one Instruction Page for each circulator; (3) provide one or more Primary Petition Signatures Pages suitable for duplication as you deem necessary (however, if the candidate or candidates are indigent, provide at least five times the number of Signatures Pages needed to contain the required number of signatures); and (4) complete all blanks in Part A of each Primary Petition Signatures Page provided. 
 
*************************************************************************************** 
 
Request for petition forms: Any person who requests a petition form must give to the Registrar of Voters before he may obtain such form (1) his own name and address, and the name, address, and position sought of each candidate for whom the petition is being obtained; and (2) a statement signed by each such candidate that he consents to be a candidate for said position. After a person has completed the foregoing requirements, the registrar of voters, before giving him the petition form, must fill in on each Petition Signatures Page the name and address of each candidate to be named therein, the position sought by each such candidate, the name of the political party holding the primary, and the date of the primary, and must also fill in the blanks on this Instruction Page. 
 
Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be provided: Upon completion of the above requirements, the Registrar of Voters shall give to the person requesting the petition form one or more Signatures Pages suitable for duplication as he deems necessary. If the candidate or candidates are indigent, the Registrar shall provide a number of pages determined by such Registrar as at least five times the number sufficient to contain the required number of signatures for a primary. The number of Signature Required is  (being at least 5% of the electors whose names appear on the active last-completed enrollment list of the party in the municipality, or such lesser number as the party rules prescribe). 
 
Time of Filing: This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party holding the primary not later than 4:00 p.m. on January 28, 2026, which is the 34th day preceding the day of the primary. 
 
General Information: A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a party- endorsed candidate) who is an enrolled member of the party within the municipality by filing with the Registrar of the party the required petition. A petition may propose as many candidates for election to the town committee as there are members to be elected to the town committee, but no more. 
 
No primary shall be held by a party for the election of a member of a town committee unless candidacies for such election numbering at least 25% of the number of town committee members to be elected by such party in such municipality are filed by persons other than party-endorsed candidates. No primary petition for town committee members shall be approved unless it contains the names of a number of candidates, other than party-endorsed candidates, equal to at least 25% of the number of town committee members to be elected. 
 
Misrepresentation Prohibited: It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally misrepresent the contents of a primary petition. Conviction of a Class D felony shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of not more than five years or a fine not exceeding $5,000, or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature of the signer; one individual may not, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, sign for another. Any person who signs a name other than his own to a primary petition or who signs a name other than his own as circulator of such a petition shall be fined not more than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one year or both. Signatures shall be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page distributed by the Registrar or on duplicates of the pages issued by the Registrar. No person shall sign as a petition signer more than once for the same candidate or candidates. Petition signatures may not be withdrawn. Only the signatures of electors whose names appear upon the last- completed enrollment list of the party holding the primary in the municipality will be counted. If the Registrar determines that an enrolled elector on the inactive registry list signed a petition giving the same address as on the inactive registry list, such name shall forthwith be placed on the active registry list and counted.    
(ED-610 Rev.12/03 - Under Secs. 9-6b, 9-368c, 9-372(12), 9-405 through 9-412, 9-421, and 9-426 [2] 
 Signers who have moved within town should be encouraged to give their birth dates, because the Registrar is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the street address on the petition is different from the street address on the enrollment list if (1) the signer is eligible to vote for the candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown on the petition is the same as the birth date on the signer's registration record. 
 
Assistance to Blind Persons in the Signing of Petitions: Any person who is blind may cause his name to be placed on a petition if an authorized agent (not the circulator) reads aloud the full text of the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the blind person consents to having his name appear on it. If a blind person is unable to write, his authorized agent may write the name of the blind person, followed by the word "by" and his own signature. 
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party in a municipality in this state who is entitled to vote. No candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition for another group of candidates for election to the town committee. No person may circulate petitions for more than the maximum number of candidates to be elected by a party to the town committee. A candidate may circulate a primary petition in which he is proposed as a candidate. Any petition page circulated in violation of these provisions of the law must be rejected by the Registrar. The circulator must be certain to have signers sign in his presence, and that his own signed statements are true, or he shall be guilty of a Class A misdemeanor, and the whole page may be rejected. A sentence of imprisonment for a Class A misdemeanor is not more than one year, and a fine for the conviction of a Class A misdemeanor shall not exceed one thousand dollars. 
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member: Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the appropriate party in the municipality in which the circulator is enrolled, attesting that the circulator is an enrolled member of such party in the municipality. This statement may be completed at any time but must be completed before the petition page is submitted for filing to the party's Registrar in the municipality of voting residence of the signers. Unless such a statement by the Registrar appears on each page so submitted, the page shall be rejected by the Registrar. 
 
Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures: The Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be completed and signed by the Circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing the page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters. The page must be rejected by the Registrar if the Statement is not completed and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer: After signing the Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures, the Circulator must appear before one of the following officers to acknowledge the statement: a judge of a court of record, clerk or deputy clerk of a court having a seal, family support magistrate, commissioner of deeds or town clerk, notary public, justice of the peace or attorney admitted to the Connecticut Bar. The acknowledgment must be completed and signed by the officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the Registrar if this is not done. 
 
"Registrar" Defined: "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political party holding a primary, and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for different voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at the last-preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of the vote of the whole municipality was moderator. 
 
***************************************************************************************

(ED-610a Rev.12/01- Under Secs. 9-6b, 9-368c, 9-372(12), 9-405 through 9-412, 9-421 and 9-426) (g:lformsled-600's\ed-610a.doc)  (5] 
 
Instruction Page for Primary Petition for Town Committee from a Political Subdivision 
 
Note to Registrars: (1) Complete on this Instruction Page, in the space provided, the total number of signatures needed for a candidacy for election to the town committee; 
(2) provide at least one Instruction Page for each circulator; (3) provide one or more Primary Petition Signatures Pages suitable for duplication as you deem necessary (however, if the candidate or candidates are indigent, provide at least five times the number of Signatures Pages needed to contain the required number of signatures); and (4) complete all blanks in Part A of each Primary Petition Signatures Page provided. 
****************************************************************************** 
Request for petition forms: Any person who requests a petition form must give to the 
Registrar of Voters before he may obtain such form (1) his own name and address, and the 
name, address, and position sought of each candidate for whom the petition is being obtained; 
and (2) a statement signed by each such candidate that he consents to be a candidate for said 
position. After a person has completed the foregoing requirements, the Registrar of Voters, 
before giving him the petition form, must fill in on each Petition Signatures Page the name 
and address of each candidate to be named therein, the position sought by each such candidate, 
the political subdivision, the name of the political party holding the primary, and the date of the 
primary, and must also fill in the blanks on this Instruction Page. 
 
Number of Petition Signatures Pages to be provided: Upon completion of the above requirements, the Registrar of Voters shall give to the person requesting the petition form one or more Signatures Pages suitable for duplication as he deems necessary. If the candidate or candidates are indigent, the Registrar shall provide a number of pages determined by such Registrar as at least five times the number sufficient to contain the required number of signatures for a primary.  The number of Signatures Required is    
(being at least 5% of the electors whose names appear on the active last- completed enrollment list of the party in the political subdivision, or such lesser number as the party rules prescribe). 
 
Time of Filing: This petition must be filed with the Registrar of Voters of the party holding the primary not later than 4:00 p.m. on January 28, 2026, which is the 34th day preceding the day of the primary. 
 
General Information: A candidacy may be filed by or on behalf of any person (except a party-endorsed candidate) who is an enrolled member of the party within the political subdivision by filing with the Registrar of the party the required petition. A petition may propose as many candidates for election to the town committee as there are members to be elected to the town committee from said political subdivision, but no more. 
 
No primary shall be held by a party for the election of a member of a town committee unless candidacies for such election numbering at least 25% of the number of town committee members to be elected by such party in such political subdivision are filed by persons other than party-endorsed candidates. No primary petition for town committee members shall be approved unless it contains the names of a number of candidates, other than party-endorsed candidates, equal to at least 25% of the number of town committee members to be elected in the political subdivision. 
 
Misrepresentation Prohibited: It is a Class D felony for any person to intentionally misrepresent the contents of a primary petition. Conviction of a Class D felony shall be punished by imprisonment for a term of not more than five years or a fine not exceeding 
$5,000, or both. 
 
Signatures: Each signature on a Petition Signatures Page must be the personal signature of the signer, and one individual may not, under any circumstances or degree of relationship, sign for another. Any person who signs a name other than his own to a primary petition or who signs a name other than his own as circulator of such a petition shall be fined not more than one hundred dollars or imprisoned not more than one year or both. Signatures shall be obtained only on the official Petition Signatures Page distributed by the Registrar or on duplicates of the pages issued by the Registrar. No person shall sign as a petition signer more than once for the same candidate or candidates. Petition signatures may not be withdrawn. Only the signatures of electors whose names appear upon the last -completed enrollment list of the party holding the primary in the political subdivision will be counted. 
  
(ED-610a Rev.12/01 - Instruction Page for Prim. Petn. -Town Comm. Political Subdivision) (g:\forms\ed-600•s\ed-610a.doc) [6] 
 If the Registrar determines that an enrolled elector on the inactive registry list signed a petition giving the same address as on the inactive registry list, such name shall forthwith be placed on the active registry list and counted. Signers who have moved within town should be encouraged to give their birth dates, because the Registrar is prohibited from rejecting any name for which the street address on the petition is different from the street address on the enrollment list if (1) the signer is eligible to vote for the candidates on the petition and (2) the signer's birth date shown on the petition is the same as the birth date on the signer's registration record. 
 
Assistance to Blind Persons in the Signing of Petitions: Any person who is blind may cause his name to be placed on a petition if an authorized agent (not the circulator) reads aloud the full text of the petition in the presence of the circulator, and the blind person consents to having his name appear on it. If a blind person is unable to write, his authorized agent may write the name of the blind person, followed by the word "by" and his own signature. 
 
Circulator: Each circulator must be an enrolled member of the party in a municipality in this state who is entitled to vote. No candidate, whether an endorsed or petitioning candidate, may circulate a petition for another group of candidates for election to the town committee in the political subdivision. No person may circulate petitions for more than the maximum number of candidates to be elected by a party to the town committee in the political subdivision. A candidate may circulate a primary petition in which he is proposed as a candidate. Any petition page circulated in violation of these provisions of the law must be rejected by the Registrar. The circulator must be certain to have signers sign in his presence, and that his own signed statements are true, or he shall be guilty of a Class A misdemeanor, and the whole page may be rejected. A sentence of imprisonment for a Class A misdemeanor is not more than one year, and a fine for the conviction of a Class A misdemeanor shall not exceed two thousand dollars. 
 
Statement by Registrar of Voters as to Circulator's Status as Enrolled Party Member: Each petition page must contain a statement signed by the Registrar of Voters of the appropriate party in the municipality in which the circulator is enrolled, attesting that the circulator is an enrolled member of such party in the municipality. This statement may be completed at any time but must be completed before the petition page is submitted for filing to the party's Registrar in the municipality of voting residence of the signers. Unless such a statement by the Registrar appears on each page so submitted, the page shall be rejected by the Registrar. 
 
Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures: The Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures, including address and number of signatures obtained, must be completed and signed by the Circulator after obtaining all the signatures and before filing the page with the appropriate Registrar of Voters. The page must be rejected by the Registrar if the Statement is not completed and signed. 
 
Certification of Acknowledging Officer: After signing the Circulator's Statement of Authenticity of Signatures, the Circulator must appear before one of the following officers to acknowledge the statement: a judge of a court of record, clerk or deputy clerk of a court having a seal, family support magistrate, commissioner of deeds or town clerk, notary public, justice of the peace or attorney admitted to the Connecticut Bar. The acknowledgment must be completed and signed by the officer taking the same, and the petition page must be rejected by the Registrar if this is not done. 
 
"Registrar" Defined: "Registrar" means the registrar of voters enrolled with the political party holding a primary, and, in each municipality where there are different registrars for different voting districts, means the registrar so enrolled in the voting district in which, at the last-preceding regular election, the presiding officer for the purpose of declaring the result of the vote of the whole municipality was moderator. 
 
********************************************************************************

Audit Procedures Manual 
 
 
 
 
 
August 2026 
 
Secretary of the State of Connecticut 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

 1

Purpose ....................................................................................................... 2

 2 
Post-Election Audit Procedures  
for Optical Scan Voting Equipment 
Purpose 
Mandatory post-election audits are to be conducted by Registrars of Voters in five percent (5%) of the voting 
districts randomly selected to participate 1. Registrars of Voters may conduct the audit by hand or 
electronically. These audits shall be open to public observation. This document outlines the procedures to 
be employed in conducting any such audits.  The primary purpose of the audit is to assess how well the 
optical scan voting machines functioned in an actual election and to ensure that votes cast using these 
machines are counted properly and accurately.  
Please note: This is not a recount where the focus is on reviewing each ballot to determine voter intent in 
rendering a final vote count. Rather, the audit tests whether the machines accurately counted ballots that 
were properly marked. Therefore, a critical part of the audit process is to separate ballots into groups: (1) 
ballots that are properly marked by voters (i.e., marked in such a way that one would expect the optical scan 
machine to be able to scan and read such ballots); and (2) ballots that one expects would not,  or may not, 
be read by the machine (e.g., a slash through the candidate oval, small dot or partially filled in oval).   
Chain-of-Custody and Counting Principle 
Whenever ballots are outside of secure storage they must, at all times, be under the supervision of at least 
two election officials of opposing parties or of opposing candidates in a primary . This includes when they 
are stored and transported from the polling place after the election; when they are transported to the audit 
facility and room; and during and after the audit until they are returned to the secure storage.  
Ballots, memory cards, and tabulators must remain under seal and in secure storage from the time of pre -
election testing until they are no longer needed for potential audits or audit investigations and the Secretary 
of the State releases them. This procedure also applies to districts not selected for audits, since the ballots 
and equipment may be needed for further investigations. Tabulators should remain under seal and in secure 
facilities, even between elections. The exceptions to these procedures are for the use of ballots and the 
equipment for pre-election testing, election, and auditing requirements. 
Counting procedures should ensure that all ballot counts, vote marks, and hash marks are read or verified 
by two eyes, that is, by two people of opposing parties or of opposing candidates in primaries. When ballots 
are counted, they should be independently counted by two people or counted jointly one at a time. When 
ballots are stacked by candidate , two people must verify that all ballots in a stack are correctly classified. 
When votes are read , a second election official should verify that the “ call” matches the mark being read. 
When hash marks are recorded, either two officials should make and reconcile hash mark sheets, or a second 
official must observe that hash marks are made for the correct candidate. 
 
1 Conn. Gen. Stat. Sec. 9-320f 
 3 
Preparing for the Audit 
Selection of Offices 
Municipal Year 
The voting districts are identified by a lottery conducted by the Secretary of the State. The selection 
process shall be open to the public. The offices on the ballot that will be subject to the audit must be 
identified by a lottery conducted by the municipal clerk. In a municipal election, three offices or twenty 
percent (20%) of the number of offices, not candidates on the ballot, whichever is greater, shall be 
selected at random by the municipal clerk at a ceremony open to the public. CGS § 9-320f(b)(3).  
Therefore, the Office of the Secretary of the State shall conduct a random drawing to select the districts 
subject to audit and the municipal clerk shall conduct a random drawing to select the offices in question. 
State Year 
The voting districts and offices will be identified by the Secretary of the State in a random drawing. The 
selection process shall be open to the public. The offices subject to the audit include all offices required to 
be audited by federal law, plus one additional office. This additional office is selected at random by the 
Secretary, but in no case will less than three offices be subject to the audit. CGS § 9-320f(b)(2). 
Therefore, the Office of the Secretary of the State shall conduct a random drawing to select the districts 
and offices in question and notify each town of the selections made. 
Primary 
The voting districts subject to an audit will be identified by the Secretary of the State in a random drawing. 
The selection process shall be public. The offices subject to the audit include all offices required to be 
audited by state law, plus one additional office, if any. In no event may less than twenty percent of the 
offices on the ballot be subject to the audit. The offices are selected in a random drawing by the municipal 
clerk. CGS § 9-320f(b)(4). 
Therefore, the Office of the Secretary of the State shall conduct a random drawing to select the voting 
districts subject to audit and the municipal clerk shall conduct a random drawing to select the offices in 
question.  For a town committee primary , only a single office  is on the ballot , so that office would be 
subject to audit without the need for a random drawing by the municipal clerk.      
Please note: Any central location used in a municipality for the counting of absentee ballots, early voting 
ballots or same-day election registration ballots shall be deemed a voting district, making central counting 
locations subject to audit. CGS § 9-320f(a). 
Selection of Audit Time and Place 
Each municipality may set its own audit date. For municipal, state, and federal elections, audits may be 
conducted not earlier than the fifteenth day after the election and not later than two business days before 
the canvass of votes. Please notify the Office of the Secretary of the State of the audit date and 
location(s) at least three business days in advance of the audit. The selected date must be published and 
made publicly available. Any number of means may be used for notice. Some examples of proper notice 
include posting notice in Town Hall and outside the Registrars’ office, posting notice on the municipality’s 
website, or newspaper advertisement. CGS § 9-320f(a). 
 4 
Selection of Audit Staff 
The Registrars of Voters are responsible f or identifying appropriate workers to conduct the audit. These 
individuals do not have to be the same individuals who worked at the polls on Election or Primary Day, but 
they should be familiar with the procedures for counting paper ballots.  Assistance for the audit may be 
solicited from  municipalities not involved in an audit. If possible, poll workers from the Republican and 
Democratic Parties in an election or from opposing slates in a primary should be equally represented. If this 
is not possible, other appropriate poll workers may be  used. The individuals selected are required to take 
an oath before performing their required duties.  
UConn’s VoTeR Lab 
Audits may be performed using the “Audit Station,” which consists of software developed in collaboration 
with the VoTeR Lab at UConn. These audits involve rescanning ballots  on document scanners, which 
produces on-screen ballot images with proposed mark interpretations that you can verify for correctness 
and electronically tally.  
Materials to be Sent to UConn in Advance: 
• 20 Blank Ballots 
• Memory cards for towns using the ES&S Machines 
  
 5 
Audit Day 
On the day of the audit, all ballot materials must be delivered to the audit location by two individuals of 
separate political parties or opposing slates in a primary, if possible. Optical scanners are not part of the 
audit and should not be brought to the audit location. Towns that elect to use the VoTeR Lab must send at 
least two people from opposing parties to conduct the audit. 
All audit procedures shall be open to public observation and administered by the Registrars of Voters. Ballots 
should remain sealed and secure, and no activities related to the audit should begin until the announced 
time of the audit. 
Conducting the Audit 
 Step 1: Confirm the Security of the Ballots 
When the audit begins, audit workers shall first examine the seal or protective tape on each of the ballot 
depository containers.  This seal number should be checked by two individuals, written down on the 
attached forms, announced publicly, and then compared to the number recorded on the moderator’s 
report. If any of the seals mentioned above have been broken or are different from those applied on Election 
or Primary night, the Registrars shall immediately notify the Legislation and Elections Division of the Office 
of the Secretary of the State.  
Step 2: Determine Which Ballots are to be Included in the Audit 
Once the balloting materials have been removed from their respective containers, all ballots shall be 
reviewed and sorted by two individuals from opposite political parties or opposing slates in a primary, if 
possible, to determine which ballots will be part of the audit.    
Note: Only  those ballots that were counted by the optical scan voting machine in the district will be 
included in the audit. That includes: 
• All ballots taken from the machine-read main section of the ballot bin; and  
• All write-in ballots taken from the machine-read, write-in section of the ballot bin. 
Absentee and SDR ballots counted by the tabulator, whether counted at the polls or centrally, are included 
in the audit.  Early Voting ballots are included in the audit.  Any ballots counted by hand  on Election or 
Primary Day are not included in the audit.    
Step 3: Count the Total Number of Ballots 
The total number of paper ballots read by each optical scan machine shall be recorded and used as a check 
to ensure that all ballots counted by the machine are included in the audit. This number can be determined 
by a separate count of the number of ballots before counting the votes, perhaps while carefully separating 
the ballots into batches to be counted by teams. This number should be the same as the number printed on 
the tabulator tape at the close of the polls.   
Step 4: Sorting the Ballots  
Each ballot shall be examined by two individuals from opposite political parties for an election audit or 
slates for a primary audit. In the case of a disagreement, the Registrars of Voters shall make the final 
decision on categorizing ballots. The ballots should be assigned to one of the following two categories: 
 6 
“Undisputed Ballots” - The first category of ballots includes those ballots that both individuals agree should 
have been read by the optical scan voting machine. In other words, a review of the ballot in question reveals 
that each oval is completely  filled in; there are no apparent problems, voter errors, unusual markings , or 
noticeable stray marks in or around any of the races to be audited. This shall include any write -in ballots 
and overvoted ballots. 
“Ballots with Questionable Votes ” - Ballots that contain problems, voter errors (e.g., check  marks in the 
candidate’s oval), stray or unusual markings in any of the races being audited should be placed in this 
category because such problems, errors , or markings may have interfered with the optical scan machine’s 
count. Here, audit workers agree that the ballots may not have been read by the optical scan voting machine. 
Please n ote: When counting an individual contest, votes should be counted as questionable only if the 
particular contest being counted has votes that are questionable on the ballot.  These questionable votes 
include those ballots where bubbles are partially filled in, even with a small dot. 
Step 5: Counting the Ballots 
Count the Total for Each Candidate: Once the ballots are separated, the audit workers shall count the votes 
for each candidate in each of the audited races on each ballot— first, from the Undisputed Ballots, i.e., 
ballots with no questionable markings; next , from the Ballots with Questionable Votes, i.e., where 
questionable markings appear for the particular race and candidate.  The audit workers will keep separate 
vote totals for each candidate from both categories of ballots. In addition, audit workers should not be 
aware of the machine totals from election night as they are counting the ballots and vote totals during the 
audit process.   
Please Note: To minimize tallying errors , it is recommended that audit workers subdivide  ballots into 
“batches” for ease of counting and detecting human tallying errors. Batch size may vary, but experience 
shows that batches of no more than 50 ballots are easiest to manage and track. It may also be useful to 
assign two workers, from different parties, if possible, to tally --make "hash marks" – for each batch, and to 
reconcile their subtotals after completing each batch of ballots. Each batch of ballots should be numbered, 
and separate tally sheets prepared for each batch. The batches and their respective tally sheets should 
remain separate to make reconciliation, if necessary, easier for audit workers. 
Never write on the ballots. Sheets or sticky notes can be clipped or stuck to each pile to identify the ballot 
and vote counts. If a notation needs to be made on an individual ballot, then that ballot should have a paper 
clipped or a sticky note attached. 
Counting Cross-Endorsed Candidates: The tabulators count votes for cross-endorsed candidates in a 
precise and special way. Your counting must conform to that method. You will note that cross-endorsed 
candidates usually have three lines on machine tapes (e.g. Jackson-Dem, Jackson-Wkf, Jackson-Unk). The 
“Unk” count means “Unknown” and corresponds to situations where a voter filled in bubbles for the 
candidate more than once. As such, when counting votes for cross-endorsed candidates, it is very effective 
to create a category/count for “Unknown” and tally votes there for instances where more than one bubble 
is filled in for the same candidate.   
 7 
Reporting Ballot Totals  
The results of the audit shall be recorded on the attached form. Do not leave any space empty.  
Incomplete forms will not be accepted. For each candidate, in each office being audited, the total count of 
“Undisputed Vote Totals” should be recorded in Column D, and the total count of “Questionable Vote 
Totals” should be recorded in Column E. Each “Questionable Vote” should be explained in the comments 
section of the audit report. Use additional space as necessary. For example: “5 instances of substantially 
incomplete bubbles.” The audit results are filed with the Secretary of the State. 
Handling Discrepancies 
If Column C (the machine total) is different from Column F (the overall count total), then an investigation as 
to the reason for the difference must be made. This could include  conducting a second hand count, if 
necessary. Any difference must be reported to the Secretary of the State.   
Small differences of one or two unexplained votes can often occur, but such differences should be verified 
by at least two counts. It is your responsibility to be thorough and comfortable that your counts of the ballots 
are accurate. If you are not confident in your counts , then continue counting and recounting until you are 
satisfied that your count result is accurate.  If the results of the audit reveal any unexplained deviations or 
errors, The University of Connecticut (UConn VoTeR Center), at the request of the Registrars  of Voters or 
Secretary of the State, shall examine the machines that produced incorrect results to determine if such 
errors were caused by the optical scan voting machine. 
Closing the Audit 
After the audit, all ballot materials shall be resealed and returned to a secure location. The optical scanners 
shall remain sealed until such time as the Secretary of the State notifies each municipality that the voting 
machine seals can be removed and the memory cards can be reprogrammed for any future election.  
Continuation of the Audit 
If the audit is to be continued in another session, take the following steps to ensure the security of the 
ballots:   
1. The ballots should be resealed in the bag and opened again at the start of the subsequent session; 
2. New seal numbers must be recorded at the end of one session and at the start of the next; 
3. Observers present and the Secretary of the State’s office should be notified at the end of the 
session of the date, time, and location of the continuation of the audit; and 
4. All audit materials should be delivered by the Registrar of Voters to a secure location during the 
interim period between audit sessions. 
  
 8 
Role of Observers 
The State of Connecticut is committed to an open, public, and transparent process . CGS § 9 -320f(a) 
specifically provides that the audit “shall be  noticed in advance and be  open to public observation.” This 
means that observers should be allowed to view every aspect of the proceedings, including being close 
enough: (1) to actually see ballots as they are being counted; (2) to see tally sheets as they are being marked 
and when they are complete; (3) to  see report forms to be sent to the Office of the Secretary of the State; 
(4) to be able to verify that the counts from tally sheets are properly added and conform to the totals on the 
report forms; (5) to be able to verify the seal number(s) and observe the condition of the seals before they 
are broken; (6) to observe the moderator’s report, machine tape, and other documents created on election 
day; and (7) to view any other documents created in the process and to ask questions of Registrars regarding 
the process. Observers should be allowed freedom of movement sufficient to enable them to view the items 
described above. 
However, it lies within the discretion of the Registrars of Voters to ensure that no observer disrupts the 
integrity or the orderliness of the process.   
 9
