6 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Introduction 
Purpose 
The purpose of this m anual is to describe the steps to be followed in receiving , handling, 
counting, and preserving absentee ballots at elections, primaries, and referenda. The procedures 
for voting and counting absentee ballots at elections must  apply as closely as possible  to 
primaries and referenda. This manual specifically states where there are differences in procedure 
for primaries or referenda. Absentee ballots are available at all elections and primaries, and for 
referenda where permitted or required by law. (Secs. 9 -133f, 9-135, as amended by P.A. 26 -42 
Sec. 1, 9-369c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 27) 
Absentee Ballot Counting Officials 
Moderator 
The moderator is the chief polling place official. The moderator must supervise the process of 
absentee ballot counting. Absentee ballots may either be counted at one central location, or at 
each polling place. If absentee ballots are counted at a central location, a central counting place 
moderator supervises the counting of absentee ballots. If absentee ballots are counted at the 
polling place, the polling place moderator supervises the counting of absentee ballots. (Sec. 9 -
150a(g), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
Registrars of Voters 
The Registrars of Voters are the municipal election officials responsible for elections, referenda, 
and their party’s primary. The registrars are jointly responsible for proper tabulator preparation, 
polling place preparation, poll worker training, and other duties as required by Connecticut 
election laws. 
Absentee Ballot Counters 
Absentee ballot counters are election officials  tasked with handling, inspecting, and counting 
absentee ballots cast by electors . The law requires all absentee ballot counters to attend a 
training session conducted by the registrar(s) of voters and the moderators. The text for the 
training session is this Manual. In towns with central counting of absentee ballots , only the 
central counting moderator must participate with the registrar(s) and absentee ballot counters. 
In towns where absentee ballots are counted at the polling place, all moderators who will serve 
at the polls at the election or primary must attend the training . Note: No spouse, parent, 
grandparent, child, or sibling of a candidate may be appointed to count absentee ballots where 
the name of the candidate appears on the ballot. (Secs. 9-147c, 9-148) 
  
   
 
7 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Administering of Oaths 
As election officials, absentee ballot counters must be sworn to the faithful performance of their 
duties. The law permits both registrars and moderators to administer oaths  to election officials, 
such as absentee ballot counters and municipal clerks. The form of the oath is as follows: 
You solemnly swear (or affirm, as the case may be) that you will faithfully discharge, 
according to law, your duties as absentee ballot counter, to the best of your ability; and 
that you will serve in this election or primary as the case may be, as an official, completely 
impartial with respect to any candidate or any political party; so help you God. 
In administering the oath, the person to whom the oath is administered should hold up their right 
hand. If a person, by reason of scruples or conscience, objects to such ceremony, or the person 
administering the oath has reason to believe that any other ceremony will be more binding upon 
the conscience of the person taking the oath, they may permit or require any other ceremony to 
be used. If, through the scruples of conscience, a person declines to take the usual form of oath, 
a solemn affirmation may be administered to them in the form of the oath prescribed, except 
that instead of the word “swear” the words  “solemnly and sincerely affirm” shall be used and 
instead of the words “so help you God” the words “upon penalty of perjury” shall be used. The 
person to whom the oath is administered should answer “I do.” (Secs. 1 -22, 1- 23, 1- 24 as 
amended by C.G.S. Supp. 2026, 1-25) 
A record of the time when , and the place where , the oath was administered to each absentee 
ballot counter must be made by the official administering the oath at the time the oath is taken.  
The record must be returned to the registrar of voters after the polls close and subsequently filed 
with the municipal clerk. See form titled “Certification of Administration of Oath  to Absentee 
Ballot Counters.” (Secs. 9-148, 9-231)   
   
 
8 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Who May Observe Absentee Ballot Counting 
Absentee Ballot Counting at Individual Polling Places 
Absentee ballot counting done at individual polling places must take place in plain view of all 
election officials and electors present in the polling room. The general public is not  allowed to 
observe the count during polling hours because access to the polling room during polling hours 
is limited to: electors casting their vote, election officials, unofficial checkers, news media,  small 
groups of students in grades  four through twelve  in attendance  for educational purposes, 
children accompanying parent/guardian electors, and runners. (Secs. 9-147a, 9-236, as amended 
by C.G.S. Supp. 2026, 9-236a, 9-261, as amended by C.G.S. Supp. 2026) 
When the count is conducted at an individual polling place, no one may reveal the result of the 
absentee ballot count that may be conducted during election day until after the close of the polls 
when the results of the vote are officially announced. (Sec. 9-147c) 
Absentee Ballot Counting at a Central Location 
Any member of the public may observe absentee ballot counting conducted at a central location, 
but they may not be so close that they can see the actual vote , nor may they interrupt the 
counting process. No candidate up for election or nomination shall be allowed to participate in 
the count, except for municipal clerks  and registrars of voters who are candidates for the same 
office, and deputy registrars of voters who are candidates for registrar of voters  and are 
performing official duties.  (Secs. 9 -147a, 9 -147c, 9 -258 as amended by P.A. 26 -42 Sec. 31)  
Further, as of January 1, 2027, Assistant Municipal Clerks, who are candidates for Municipal Clerk, 
may perform their official duties at the polls. 
When the count is conducted at one central location, no one may reveal the result of the 
absentee ballot count that may be conducted during election day until after the close of polls 
when the results of the vote are officially announced. (Sec. 9-147c) 
  
   
 
9 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Types of Absentee Ballots 
Regular Absentee Ballots 
Any elector may choose to exercise their right to vote by applying for an absentee ballot. They 
no longer need to cite a specific reason before obtaining one. All candidates and questions to be 
voted upon are printed on the regular absentee ballot.  (Sec. 9-135, as amended by P.A. 26 -42 
Sec. 1) 
Official Blank Absentee Ballots 
In regular elections only, special early official blank absentee ballots (ED-81a) are available 90 
days before the regular election to all members of the armed forces, as well as their spouses and 
dependents living where they are stationed, who due to military contingencies cannot follow the 
regular 31-day absentee ballot procedure. Because candidates are not known 90 days before an 
election, a list of candidates is not included in a blank ballot. The list of candidates and questions 
will be sent to the elector as soon as it is available.  (Sec. 9-153e, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 
14) 
Official blank absentee ballots are also available as soon as a list of candidates and questions to 
be voted upon are available to electors residing or expecting to be travelling outside the United 
States and to servicemembers as well as their spouses and dependents living within or outside 
the United States. The list of candidates and questions to be voted upon is usually made available 
approximately 45 days before an election or primary . Occasionally, the list of candidates and 
questions is not available at the 45 -day mark. In that case, the blank ballot shall still be sent to 
the elector at the 45-day mark and the list of candidates and questions shall be sent to the elector 
as soon as it is available. (Sec. 9-153f, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 15) 
Electors using the official blank absentee ballot cast their vote by writing in the names of the 
candidates and offices for which they are voting. Electors in an election may also vote on the 
questions as prescribed on the ballot.  
  
   
 
10 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Official Overseas Ballots 
Qualified United States citizens residing outside the United States may vote in federal elections 
for candidates for the offices of President, Vice President, Presidential Electors, United States 
Senator, and Representative in Congress by using the Official Overseas Ballot (ED -71). Such 
persons may also vote in federal primaries for candidates for the offices of United States Senator 
and Representative in Congress using the Official Primary Overseas Ballot (ED -71Pr). Such 
persons may also vote in Presidential Preference Primaries using special envelopes and the 
Official Presidential Preference Absentee Ballot (ED-2PrP). (Secs. 9-158a et. Seq.) 
Additionally, qualified United States citizens who expect to be traveling on or before election day, 
and are registered Connecticut voters, may vote absentee for all  offices being contested at an 
election or primary. (Sec. 9-153f, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 15) 
Presidential Ballots 
Qualified former residents may vote in  presidential elections only , and only for the office of 
presidential electors using the Official Presidential Ballot (ED-31). (Secs. 9-158a et. Seq.) 
Note: The Official Blank Absentee Ballots, Overseas Ballots, and Presidential Ballots should be 
counted in the same manner as regular absentee ballots. 
Electronically-Issued Ballots 
Pursuant to the Uniformed and Overseas Citizens Absentee Voting Act (U OCAVA) and relevant 
state law, the State of Connecticut developed procedures for transmitting blank ballots to voters 
by mail or, upon the request of the elector, electronically. 
These ballots can be legally sent to an elector by electronic mail. In this case, the elector will not 
have an envelope in which to return their ballot. Instead, the elector will receive and print an 
Official Blank Absentee Ballot, the list of candidates, and a certification page. After filling out their 
ballot, the elector will fold and insert the ballot and the certification page in an envelope and 
return them to the municipal clerk of the municipality where the elector is registered to vote. 
Note: Step 4 of the “Absentee Ballot Counting Steps” in this Manual refers to the rejection of an 
absentee ballot if it is not returned in the proper  envelope. Because these ballots are issued 
electronically, the elector will not receive a return envelope. The certification statement included 
with the ballot is intended to be used in lieu of a  return envelope and must be completed and 
returned in order for the ballot to be counted. (Secs. 9 -153e, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 14, 
9-153f, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 15) 
  
   
 
11 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Delivery and Receipt of Absentee Ballots 
Delivery Time and Time for Counting Absentee Ballots 
Individual Polling Places 
If a town is counting absentee ballots at the individual polling place, and the type of election is a 
regular election, primary, or referendum, ballots must be delivered by the municipal clerk to the 
registrars of voters by 8:00 a.m., and after the close of the polls at 8:00 p.m.   
If there is a special election or referendum, the ballots must be deliv ered by 8:00 a.m. or a 
mutually agreed-upon time be fore 4:00 p.m.  Ballots may also be delivered at 6:00 p.m. at the 
option of the registrar (s). If the optional 6:00 p.m. delivery is used and there are no ballots left 
to be delivered at 8:00 p.m., the 8:00 p.m. delivery need not be conducted.  
The actual counting of the ballots can be performed at any time as designated by the registrars 
of voters, even if the ballots are delivered at various times during the day to the counting 
locations. (Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7)   
If the municipality employs absentee ballot counters at the polling place , the absentee ballot 
counters must arrive at the counting location at least 15 minutes before the deadline for clerks 
to deliver the ballots. (P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
Central Counting Locations 
If a town is conducting central counting, and the type of election is a regular election, primary, or 
referendum, the ballots shall be checked and delivered by the registrars of voters to the counting 
locations by 8:00 a.m., and after the polls close at 8:00 p.m.  
If there is a special election or referendum, the ballots must be delivered by 8:00 a.m. or a 
mutually agreed upon time before 4:00  p.m. Ballots may also be delivered at 6:00 p.m.  at the 
option of the registrar(s).  
The actual counting of the ballots  can be performed at any time as designated by the registrars 
of voters, even if the ballots are delivered at various times during the day to the counting 
locations. (Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7)   
The absentee ballot  counters must arrive at the absentee ballot counting location at least 15 
minutes before the deadline for the clerk to deliver the absentee ballots. (P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
Receipt of Ballots (Endorsements) 
As absentee ballots are received by the municipal  clerk, the clerk must endorse each envelope. 
Each endorsement must include the date, the time of receipt, the method of receipt, and the 
clerk’s signature . The method of receipt should indicate whether the absentee ballot was  
returned via the United States Postal Service or another commercial carrier, deposited in a secure 
   
 
12 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
drop box, returned in person by the elector, or returned in person by a designee or immediate 
family member of an elector. If the method of receipt was via drop box, the drop box location or 
assigned drop box number should be notated. (Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
The municipal clerk must also execute an affidavit attesting to the accuracy of each endorsement 
and give the affidavit to the head moderator at the close of the polls. The head moderator, at the 
time of their receipt of this affidavit, must sign the affidavit, noting the time they received it. The 
affidavit must be returned to the clerk when the counting is complete. See form titled “Affidavit 
of Endorsement of Absentee Ballot Envelopes.” (Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
  
   
 
13 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Presorting, Prechecking, and Curing of Absentee 
Ballots 
Presorting 
Starting on the  day after the  first day that absentee ballots are issued,  all absentee ballots 
received by the municipal clerk at or before 11:00 a.m. on any weekday shall be sorted into  the 
proper voting districts.  
Absentee ballots received by the municipal  clerk after 11:00 a.m. on the last  day before the 
election, primary, or referendum that is not a Sunday or a legal holiday, and before 6:00 p.m. on 
the day of the election, primary, or referendum shall be presorted by the municipal clerk in the 
same manner. 
The municipal clerk will retrieve ballots from the secure drop box each business day, beginning  
on the first day of absentee voting set issuance until the close of polls.  
Any absentee ballots received after 6:00 p.m. shall be sorted into their proper voting districts and 
delivered to the registrars of voters after the polls close at 8:00 p.m. for checking by the absentee 
ballot counters. (Sec. 9-140b, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 6, Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 
26-42 Sec. 7) 
Prechecking  
Note: All names must be prechecked in the manner described below, except for Absentee Ballots 
Issued on the Basis of Faxed Applications and Federal Write-in Absentee Ballots. 
During the presorting period, the registrar(s) may check daily the names of the absentee electors 
on the official checklist by marking “absentee” or “A” next to the elector’s name. All  ballots 
received by 11:00 a.m. on the last weekday before the election or primary must be presorted and 
prechecked in this manner. After the presorting and prechecking is completed , the municipal 
clerk must seal the unopened ballots in a package, so that no one may open the package without 
the clerk becoming aware, and keep them in a safe place until they are delivered to the registrars 
by 8:00 a.m.  on the day of the election, primary, or referendum. In the case of special elections 
or referenda, the clerk and registrars may mutually agree to other times, before 4:00 p.m.    
Like in the earlier section titled “Receipt of Ballots (Endorsements) ,” the municipal clerk and 
registrar(s) must execute an affidavit of delivery and receip t of the presorted and prechecked 
absentee ballots that have been delivered. The affidavit of delivery and receipt must be executed 
by the legally prescribed times of 8:00 a.m., at 6:00 p.m. if there is an optional delivery at that 
time, and after the polls close at 8:00 p.m. if a count is required at that time. See form titled 
Affidavit of Delivery and Receipt of Absentee Ballot.” (Sec. 9-140c, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 
7)
  
   
 
14 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Curing 
State law now provides for an opportunity to “cure” an absentee ballot that is returned without 
a signature on the  return envelope. Absentee ballots can be cured by the elector signing the 
return envelope in the presence of the municipal clerk at the clerk’s office. Absentee ballots can 
be cured until polls close on election night. If the ballot is not cured, the ballot is rejected.  
When a municipal  clerk finds that an absentee ballot envelope has not been signed, the clerk 
shall make their best efforts to contact the elector and inform them that the  elector may come 
into the municipal clerk’s office to sign the envelope.  The clerk may call the elector  over the 
telephone, email the voter, or use other best efforts to make contact with the elector. The clerk 
should create and store a record of the date, time, and method of any and all attempts that were 
made. The elector may come into the clerk’s office, wherein the clerk shall produce the applicable 
unsigned absentee ballot envelope and have the voter sign.  The clerk should not request any 
additional identification from the voter.  (P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
Additionally, the municipal clerk will make a designation of “CE” in CVRS. “CE” stand for “cure 
eligible.” Any elector with such designation may vote in person as an alternative to appearing 
before the municipal clerk. If such voter votes in person, the moderator will contact the municipal 
clerk, and the municipal clerk shall reject the absentee ballot.   
 
  
   
 
15 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Special Procedures for Certain Absentee Ballots 
Overview 
This section covers the special procedures for handling, checking, and counting Absentee Ballots 
Issued on the Basis of Faxed Applications, Overseas Ballots in Federal Elections, Federal Write-in 
Absentee Ballots (FWABs), and Provisional Ballots . The procedures outlined for handling, 
checking, and counting the types of absentee ballots in this section differs from the general  
absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the upcoming “Procedure for Counting Absentee 
Ballots” section. 
Absentee Ballots Issued on the Basis of Faxed Applications 
Regular Ballots in Return Envelopes Displaying Unique Ballot Identification Numbers 
The municipal clerk must keep a list of names and addresses of all persons to whom ballots were 
issued on the basis of a faxed ED- 3 application. When an ED -3 with the elector’s signature is 
received, the municipal clerk crosses the name off the list. On the day before the election, if the 
ED-3 with the elector’s signature has not  been received, the registrars of voters must not  
precheck the name of the elector off the list. The municipal clerk must hold these ballots and 
note on each unique  ballot identification number ed envelope information similar to the 
following: “Not prechecked because faxed ED -3 with elector’s signature was no t received. Hold 
until 8:00 p.m. on election night to be checked off the list and counted or rejected.” 
At 8:00 p.m. on election night, the absentee ballot counters shall  receive these ballots and the 
list, prepared by the municipal clerk, of electors who have not returned an application with their 
signature by 8:00 p.m.  The counters will check off the names of the electors on the registry (if 
the elector has not already voted in person) and the counters will check with the municipal clerk 
(or the clerk’s list) to confirm the application with the elector’s signature was received by 8:00 
p.m. If the application with the elector’s signature was  not received by 8:00 p.m. , the counters 
shall open the unique ballot identification numbered envelope to determine whether  the 
application with the elector’s signature is inside, all the while making every effort to preserve the 
privacy of the vote. If the elector’s signature is inside and the application was properly completed, 
the counters shall note on the unique  ballot identification numbered envelope that the 
application with the elector’s signature was received inside and shall count the ballot. The ballot 
shall be counted by following the general absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the 
upcoming “Procedure for Counting Absentee Ballots” section. 
If the application with the elector’s signature is not inside the envelope or has not been properly 
completed, the counters shall put everything from that elector back into the return envelope  
bearing a unique ballot identification number and mark the envelope with information similar to 
the following: “Rejected—Faxed ED-3 application with the elector’s signature not received and 
properly completed by 8:00 p.m. on election night.” 
  
   
 
16 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Federal Elections —Overseas Ballot Returned in Unique  Ballot Identification Numbered 
Envelope 
The municipal clerk must keep a list of the names and addresses of all persons to whom Official 
Overseas Ballots were issued on the basis of  a faxed Federal Post Card Application (FPCA) or 
Faxed ED-70 application. When the FPCA or ED -70 with the elector’s signature is received, the 
municipal clerk crosses the elector’s name off the list. On the day before the election, registrars 
of voters must not precheck the name of an elector off the list if the voter has not submitted the 
original wet ink application. The municipal clerk must hold these ballots and note on each unique 
ballot identification numbered envelope , information similar to the following: “Not prechecked 
because faxed FPCA (or ED-70) with the original signature was not received. Hold until 8:00 p.m. 
on election night to be checked off the list to be counted or rejected.” Note Exception: If the 
faxed FPCA was sent by a member of the armed services, or a spouse or dependent of a member 
of the armed services, or an elector temporarily living overseas, such faxed FPCA is sufficient 
without an original signature  and the registrar of voters should precheck the name off the list. 
This exception only applies when the faxed FPCA is submitted solely to request an absentee ballot 
and is not being used to either register to vote, change their address, or change their pa rty 
affiliation). It does not apply to the ED-70 or federal/presidential only voters.   
At 8:00 p.m. on election night, the counters will receive these ballots and a list prepared by the 
municipal clerk indicating those overseas electors who have not returned an application with the 
elector’s signature by 8:00 p.m. The counters will check off the names of the overseas electors 
on the registry  list and check with the municipal clerk to see if the FPCA or ED -70 with the 
elector’s signature was received by 8:00 p.m. If the application with the original signature was 
received, the counters shall note this on the unique ballot identification numbered envelope and 
shall count the ballot by following the general absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the 
upcoming “Procedure for Counting Absentee Ballots” section. 
If the FPCA or ED-70 with the elector’s signature has not been received by 8:00 p.m., the counters 
shall open the unique ballot identification numbered envelope to see if the application with the 
original signature is inside. If the elector’s signature is inside and the application was properly 
completed, the counters shall note on the unique ballot identification numbered envelope that 
the application with the elector’s signature was received inside and shall count the ballot by 
following the general absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the upcoming “Procedure 
for Counting Absentee Ballots” section. 
Similarly, if the ballot was sent electronically,  the counters shall open the envelope to see if the 
application with the original signature is inside. If the elector’s signature is inside and the 
application was properly completed, the counters shall note on the envelope that the application 
with the elector’s signature was received inside and shall count the ballot by following the 
general absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the upcoming “Procedure for Counting 
Absentee Ballots” section. 
If there was no faxed FPCA and no faxed ED -70 received by the municipal clerk, but an Official 
Overseas Ballot was issued on the basis of a “Direction by the Registrar of Voters” (ED -12), then 
the original FPCA or ED-70 must be received prior to the day of the election. The municipal clerk 
   
 
17 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
must hold such overseas ballots to be counted at the 6:00 p.m. or 8:00 p.m. count. If the 
municipal clerk does not receive the original FPCA or ED -70 by the day before the election, the 
municipal clerk must mark each envelope with information similar to the following: “Rejected—
Original Application Not Received on Time.” (C.G.S. Sec. 9-153d) 
   
 
18 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Federal Elections—Counting Federal Write-in Absentee Ballots 
Federal Write-in Absentee Ballots (FWABs) are absentee ballots that are received from anywhere 
outside the United States, or APO/FPO addresses. APO and FPO addresses are military addresses 
for service  members who are stationed overseas or in remote locations. APOs and FPOs are 
considered to be outside of the United States. 
The municipal clerk shall hold all FWABs for the 8:00 p.m. count. If an individual who returned an 
FWAB lists a residence in town and is not a registered elector by 5:00 p.m. on the day before the 
federal election, the municipal clerk shall give  the name and address of the individual to the 
registrars who shall add the elector’s name to the list of Overseas Voters to be attached to the 
checklist for the proper voting district. (Sec. 9-158j) 
If a full Connecticut absentee ballot is returned by 8:00 p.m. on election day by an individual who 
also voted an FWAB and was a registered elector by 5:00 p.m. on the day before the federal 
election, the municipal clerk shall write on the envelope of the FWAB information similar to the 
following: “Rejected—Full ballot returned on time.” At 8:00 p.m. on election day, the counters 
shall not open FWAB envelopes , because the full Connecticut ballot shall be checked off on the 
checklist and the counters shall count all offices voted on in the full absentee ballots. (Secs. 9 -
153b, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 12, 9-153d, 42 USC 1973ff) 
At 8:00 p.m. on election day, the municipal clerk shall deliver all FWABs (sorted by voting district) 
to the registrars to deliver them to the counters. The counters shall  treat all FWABs as Overseas 
Ballots and list them on the Moderator’s Return as Overseas Ballots. If by 8:00 p.m. on election 
day the municipal clerk does not receive a full absentee ballot from an individual who returned 
an FWAB, and if the FWAB has not been rejected, then the counters shall check off the FWAB on 
the checklist and count the ballot in the FWAB for federal office s only. The counters shall not 
open any FWABs which have been rejected by the municipal  clerk and shall report them on the 
“Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count” of the Moderator’s Return in the “Total Ballots Rejected” 
column. 
  
   
 
19 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Provisional Ballots 
If an individual applies for an absentee ballot, and pursuant to the Help America Vote Act 
(HAVA), they are required to submit identification with their absentee ballot, there will be an 
asterisk (*) next to the elector’s name on the official registry list. State law requires the town 
clerk to send any voter whose name is marked with an asterisk a second envelope containing 
instructions for returning their identification in the preaddressed envelope provided. The town 
clerk will provide the registrars with any voter identification received before Election Day so 
that the asterisk next to the voter’s name can be removed. Returned absentee ballots for 
voters who did not submit a copy of their identification to the town clerk will be given to the 
counters, who will examine the contents of the return envelope to determine whether the 
required identification is included. 
 
On election day, the counters will receive these absentee ballots in the same manner that  they 
receive other absentee ballots. The counters will check off the names of the electors on the 
registry if the elector has not already voted in person. 
If there is still an asterisk next to the name of an elector on the registry list who has submitted 
an absentee ballot, the counters will open the unique ballot identification numbered envelope 
to see if a copy of  the elector’s  identification is inside. If so, the counters shall remove the 
identification without opening the ballot and examine the identification to see if the information 
contained on the unique ballot identification numbered envelope matches the information on 
the identification. If the identification is sufficient, the counters shall note on the unique  ballot 
identification numbered envelope that the identification is sufficient, and then the ballot shall be 
counted by following the general absentee ballot counting procedure outlined in the upcoming 
“Procedure for Counting Absentee Ballots” section. Additionally, a copy of the identification shall 
be forwarded to the registrar of voters.
 
If the required identification is not inside the envelope, the counters shall put everything from 
the elector back into the unique ballot identification numbered envelope and mark with 
information similar to the following: “Rejected as an Absentee Ballot.” Once rejected as an 
absentee ballot, the ballot will be considered a provisional ballot for federal offices only. (Sec. 9-
150a, as amended by P.A. 26 -1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26 -42 Sec. 10) . The counters shall 
record the elector’s name, address, date of birth, and unique ballot identification number of the 
absentee ballot on the form titled “Provisional Ballot Inventory Form”. The absentee ballot shall 
then be placed in the Provisional Ballot Depository Envelope. 
At the close of polls, the counters shall place all absentee ballots that will be considered as 
Provisional Ballots in the Provisional Ballot Depository Envelope and forward such materials to 
the moderator of the polling place.  The moderator shall then seal the Provisional Ballot 
Depository Envelope, execute the form titled “Affidavit of Delivery and Receipt of Provisional 
Ballots” found on page 40 of this Manual, and deliver such materials to the registrar of voters. 
Note: If an absentee ballot is rejected for a reason other than a lack of identification as required 
by HAVA (such as two ballots in one ED -8 Return Envelope), the absentee ballot is rejected 
outright and shall not be treated as a provisional ballot. 
   
 
20 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Procedure for Counting Absentee Ballots 
Overview 
This section outlines the steps to follow when counting absentee ballots. This section also 
provides a series of visual examples to help illustrate common absentee ballot marking errors 
that a counter may come across when counting absentee ballots. 
Absentee ballots shall be delivered to the counting location at  the prescribed time on election, 
primary, or referendum day. Prior to counting the ballot s, the absentee ballot counters must 
report to the polling place or central counting location , at least 15 minutes before the clerk 
delivers the ballots to the registrar,  to review ballots. The following procedure for counting 
absentee ballots must be strictly adhered to. Election laws describe the manner and method by 
which absentee ballots are to be received, handled, counted, and preserved. This Manual should 
be referred to by absentee ballot counters during the absentee ballot counting process to 
receive, handle, count, and preserve absentee ballots according to Connecticut election laws. 
Absentee Ballot Counting Steps 
Step 1. The counters report to the moderator the total number of absentee ballots received. This 
total includes Official Blank Absentee Ballots. If applicable, counters shall also report the total 
number of Overseas Ballots and Presidential Ballots received. The moderator will record the 
totals on the form titled “Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count”  as part of the “Moderator’s 
Return.” (Sec. 9-150a(c), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
Step 2.  Except for ballots that have already been prechecked by the registrars, the absentee 
ballot counter must check the absentee elector’s name and address as it appears on the return  
envelope against the elector’s name as it appears on the official checklist and mark “absentee” 
or “A” next to the elector’s name on the list. If the elector’s name has already been checked off 
the checklist as voting in person, mark the envelope received  with information similar to the 
following: “Rejected—Elector Voted in Person.” (Sec. 9-140c(i), as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
 Step 3. The counters shall check each return envelope to be sure that it has been endorsed by 
the municipal clerk. The endorsement must show the date, the time the clerk received the ballot, 
the method of receipt, and the clerk’s signature. If a  return envelope does not comply with this 
requirement, the ballot cannot be counted. For example, if the envelope is merely stamped with 
a generic date stamp and contains no indication by hand, stamp facsimile, or printed name and 
title that it was received by the municipal clerk, the ballot cannot be counted. If the ballot cannot 
be counted, mark the envelope received with information similar to  the following: “Rejected—
Clerk’s Endorsement Incomplete.” Place the rejected ballots in a depository envelope. (Secs. 9 -
150a(c), as amended by P.A. 26 -1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26 -42 Sec. 10, 9- 140c(a) as 
amended by P.A. 26 -42 Sec. 7; Dombkowski v. Messier, 164 Conn. 204 (1972); In Re Election of 
U.S. Representative for the Second Congressional District, 231 Conn. 602, 651 (1994))   
In municipalities with more than one polling place, and where absentee ballots are counted at 
the individual polling places, and an elector’s name is absent from the official checklist for that 
   
 
21 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
polling location where their ballot was received, the polling place moderator must contact the 
registrar(s) to find out if the elector’s name is on the official checklist at another polling place in 
town. If so, the ballot must be counted at the correct polling place. Arrangements must be made 
with the registrar(s) to deliver the ballot to the  correct polling place  to be counted. (Secs. 9 -
140c(b), as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7, 9-140c(c), as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7, 9-140c(i), 
as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
If an elector’s name is not on the official checklist in any polling place in town, the ballot cannot 
be counted. Mark the envelope received with information similar to the following: “Rejected —
Elector’s Name not on Official Checklist.” Place the rejected ballot in the depository envelope 
containing other rejected ballots. (Sec. 9-140c(c), as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
Step 4. If the unique ballot identification numbered return envelope issued to the elector is not 
returned with the elector’s ballot, mark the envelope received with information similar to the 
following: “Rejected— Return Envelope Not with Ballot.” Note: See the earlier section 
“Electronically Issued Military and Overseas Ballots ” that covers absentee ballots that  were 
issued without a unique ballot identification numbered envelope but should still be accepted  if 
their certification page is completed. (Sec. 9-140a, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 5) 
Step 5. Printed on each envelope is a statement which must be signed by the elector. If the 
statement is not signed, the ballot cannot  be counted. The envelope shall be marked with 
information similar to the following: “Rejected —Elector’s Voted Statement Not Signed.” Note: 
An elector who is unable to write or sign their name can have a designee write the name of the 
elector, followed the word “by” and then the designee’s own signature. (Secs. 9 -150a(d) as 
amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10, 9-140a, as amended by P.A. 26-
42 Sec. 5) Please note: While the lack of a signature is sufficient reason not to count an absentee 
ballot, the envelope will also have a line for the printed name and the date. If either or both of 
these lines are not filled in, the ballot must still be counted. It is only the signature that is 
positively required. 
Step 6. Place the removed envelopes in the depository envelope and place the envelopes marked 
“Rejected” in the same depository envelope. Report to the moderator the number of envelopes 
marked “Rejected” for use in completing the form titled “Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count” 
as a part of the “Moderator’s Return.” (Sec. 9 -150a(f), as amended by P.A. 26 -1 Sec. 90, as 
amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
Step 7. Remove the ballots. If the ballot is in a privacy sleeve, remove the ballot from the privacy 
sleeve. No, ballot should be rejected if it is not in a privacy sleeve. Leave the ballots folded so 
that no markings can be seen. This is required to preserve the secrecy of the vote. 
Step 8. Seal the depository envelope with nonreusable tape. The tape must cover the depository 
envelope flap. The depository envelope should state the time of the count and the voting district 
(in towns that are divided into voting districts). The absentee ballot counters shall endorse their 
names and the envelope’s contents on the depository envelope. (Sec. 9 -150a(f), as amended by 
P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
   
 
22 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Note: As mentioned earlier in the “Provisional Ballots” section, an absentee ballot that was 
rejected due to a failure by the elector to return a copy of their identification with their ballot, as 
required by HAVA, will be placed in the Provisional Ballot Depository Envelope. The ballot will be 
considered as a Provisional Ballot by the registrars of voters after the close of polls.  
Step 9. Shuffle the folded ballots and then unfold them. 
Step 10. Set aside ballots to be hand counted. 
A. Ballots That Must be Hand Counted 
The following ballots must be hand counted: Official Blank Absentee Ballots (ED-81a, ED-
8Pr), Overseas Ballots (ED -71, ED -71Pr in federal elections and primaries only), and 
Presidential Ballots (ED-31).  
B. Ballots With Obvious Marking Errors 
Quickly inspect the ballots before feeding them into the tabulator. Any ballots which 
obviously cannot be processed by the tabulator (mutilated ballots, ovals filled in with 
improper ink, etc.) should be set aside for hand counting. Also, set aside ballots  which 
contain markings that will obviously result in lost votes (ovals on the ballot marked with 
a check mark or an “X” rather than filling in the oval completely; circling the candidate or 
party name rather than filling in the oval completely). The purpose of this brief inspection 
is to spot obvious errors, not to substitute a hand count for tabulator processing. All 
offices and questions will have to be hand counted on these ballots that have been set 
aside. 
C. Offices Elected from Part of a Town 
If a wrong district ballot was improperly issued to an elector in a town where there are 
different candidates for the same office in different parts of the town, the ballot should 
be completely hand counted and only the races that the elector is entitled to vote for 
should be counted. 
D. Stickered Races 
If the municipal clerk has notified election officials that a candidate’s name has changed 
since the first absentee ballot was issued and that the voting tabulator has been 
programmed not to count that office on the absentee ballots, you must hand count the 
ballots for that office only. The hand count must take place before the absentee ballots 
are fed through the tabulator. These situations are called “Stickered Races.” 
Step 11. Count the number of absentee ballots to be tabulator counted, wholly or in part, and 
give the number to the moderator to enter on the “Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count” as part 
of the “Moderator’s Return.” Feed these ballots into the tabulator for processing. If the ballot is 
rejected by the tabulator, follow the instructions on the display, as modified by the following 
instructions: 
A. Overvote 
   
 
23 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
If the tabulator indicates that a ballot contains an overvote, check the ballot to be sure 
that there is in fact an overvote and not either a stray mark through one of the ovals (see 
Example 3) or a cross-endorsement which has been voted upon on multiple rows. Unless 
the ballot clearly shows an attempt by the elector to cast more than one vote for an office 
(other than a cross -endorsement), the ballot should be set aside to hand count for all 
offices. Subtract from the number of absentee ballots counted by the tabulator.  
 
If the ballot does contain a true overvote, reinsert the ballot, press and hold the “yes” key 
at the same time to perform an override. When the tabulator performs an override, it 
does not count any votes for the office in which an overvote occurs, but it does count all 
other properly marked votes. 
 
B. Blank Ballot 
If the tabulator displays a message saying that a ballot is a “blank ballot”, it usually means 
that the entire ballot is marked in such a way that the tabulator can read no votes 
whatsoever (the ballot may be marked with improper ink or the ovals for a can didate’s 
name are check marked instead of filled in). Subtract the number of absentee ballots  to 
be tabulator counted and set aside these ballots to be hand counted for all offices. 
 
Note: These types of “blank” or unreadable ballots are different from Official Blank Ballots 
that have been mentioned throughout this Manual. Official Blank Ballots are special 
absentee ballots that are issued before full lists of candidates are available. Review the  
example of an Official Blank Ballot. 
 
There is another situation, wherein the voter has returned an absentee ballot with no 
votes marked on it. In this case, the tabulator will give the counters the option to cast the 
ballot without a vote. 
Step 12. The rule for hand counting ballots is that the intent of the electo r governs. If the 
elector’s ballot is properly marked, then the elector’s intent is clear. However, many ballots are 
not properly marked. The following are a series of rules and examples of said rules for counting 
correctly or incorrectly cast absentee ballots. If a ballot to be counted resembles one of the 
following example ballots presented, the absentee ballot must be counted according to the rule 
and the illustration of the rule seen in the example. 
If none of the example ballots are representative of the ballot to be counted, the absentee ballot 
counters must submit the ballot to the moderator and the moderator will make the decision as 
to whether the ballot may be counted or rejected. If the moderator is of the opinion that the 
ballot is marked for the purpose of providing a means of identifying the elector who cast i t, the 
ballot may be rejected. However, the Connecticut Supreme Court upheld a moderator’s decision 
to count an absentee ballot for the office of Representative in Congress where the elector made 
a correction on their vote for Governor and noted, “Please register my vote for Scott instead of 
   
 
24 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Rowland,” and signed their name. ( In Re Election of U.S. Representative for the Second 
Congressional District, 231 Conn. 602, 657 (1994). The moderator must record the question and 
their decision in writing on the back of the ballot and return the ballot to the absentee ballot 
counters. (C.G.S. Sec. 9-150a(k), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 
10, C.G.S. Sec. 9-150a(l), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
A. Properly Marked Absentee Ballot 
To cast a regular absentee ballot in a primary or election, the elector must fill in the oval 
corresponding to the name of each candidate for whom they wish to vote. In Example 1, 
a vote is counted for George Washington and Dwight Eisenhower.

In the case of an office for which the elector may vote for two or more candidates, an 
elector may vote for any combination of candidates, up to the maximum for which the 
elector may vote, even if the candidates are opposite each other in the same column. The 
elector may vote for the full number of candidates for whom they are entitled to vote, or 
less than the full number, but they cannot exceed that number. In Example 2, a vote is 
counted for Lillian Hellman, Graham Greene, and Nathaniel Hawthorne  since the elector 
was allowed to vote for any 3 candidates.

B. Overvote 
When an elector casts votes for more candidates for a particular office than the number 
for which they are entitled to vote for that office, the vote is void for that office. In 
Example 3, the elector cast two votes for the office of First Selectman and fo ur votes for 
the Office of Board of Education. Their vote is void for both offices  since they voted for  
more candidates than they were entitled to and a vote is counted for Seneca for the Office 
of Selectman since they voted for the proper number of candidates that they were 
entitled to.

The voting tabulator is programmed to reject any such ballot and to display a message 
indicating that there has been an overvote on that ballot. 
 
Sometimes the tabulators can interpret stray marks, attempted erasures, stains, or 
foreign material in the oval as an overvote. If the elector were voting in person at the 
polls, they would have the opportunity to re -mark their ballot to correct the problem. 
However, with absentee ballots, the elector is not present to correct the problem . To 
avoid unnecessary lost votes and to give effect to the intent of the voter, whenever an 
“overvote” message is displayed while processing absentee ballots, the election official 
processing the absentee ballot should inspect it to be sure that there is a genuine 
overvote, which is  an attempt by the elector to vote for more candidates tha n allowed 
for an office. If there is a genuine overvote, such as the one seen in Example 3, the 
override procedure described in Step 11A should be used. Unless you are sure that it is a 
genuine overvote, the ballot should be set aside for hand counting. 
 
Exception: Cross Endorsement 
When a candidate has been cross endorsed, their name will appear twice on the ballot 
for the same office along each party’s line for which they have been endorsed. If an 
elector votes for that same candidate for that same office twice, the voting tabulator will 
read this as an overvote. You will need to hand count these ballots  and register a single 
vote for the candidate. In Example 3-A, a single vote is recorded for Sophocles since they 
   
 
27 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
were cross endorsed, and no votes are recorded for the Office of Board of Education since 
there is an overvote.

C. Write-in Votes 
Registered Write-in Candidates: Write-in votes for candidates registered with the office 
of the Secretary of the State (or with the municipal clerk in cases such as Representative 
Town Meeting members) must also be counted and recorded on the return form titled 
“Write-in Votes for Registered Write-in Candidates” as part of the “Moderator’s Return.” 
The registrar of voters will supply the moderator with a list of registered write -in 
candidates, which the moderator will give to the absentee ballot counters. Note: Write-
in votes are permitted in elections only. 
 
What the Tabulator Will Recognize: For a vote for a registered write -in candidate on a 
regular absentee ballot to be recognized by the tabulator, the elector must both mark the 
oval in the “write -in” box for the office and  write in the candidate’s name in the 
appropriate write-in box. At the close of the polls (or earlier if there is Central Counting 
of absentee ballots), all write -in ballots will be identified by the tabulator and an image 
of the write in vote will appear in the report. This image, and only the image of the office 
in which the write-in oval is marked is hand counted. In Example 4 , the ballot containing 
the write -in vote for Alexander Bell would be hand counted for the Office of First 
Selectman.

oval for First Selectman is  not marked, the tabulator will have no way of telling that 
someone’s name was written in and it will not record a ny vote for the office of First 
Selectman. Additionally, if the oval is not marked, the ballot cannot be identified as a 
write-in vote by the tabulator and will not be hand counted after the close of polls. 
Instead, the vote for Alexander Bell would be lost, even if they were a registered write-in 
candidate. 
 
Write-in Votes for Candidates on the Ballot: Write-in votes on absentee ballots for 
candidates whose names appear on the ballot must also be counted. The Connecticut 
Supreme Court has ruled that a write -in of the number and letter of a candidate’s  vote 
indicator (oval) on the voting tabulator must be counted if the ballot is hand counted. (In 
Re Election of U.S. Representative for the Second Congressional District , 231 Conn. 602, 
667 (1994)). However, an elector may never vote more than once for one candidate for 
the same office. If the elector fills in the oval to vote  for William Johnson who is on the 
ballot and also writes him in for the same office and fills in the oval in the write -in row, 
only one  vote should be counted for this candidate. Do not  treat this situation as an 
overvote. If the absentee ballot has been fed into the tabulator without being looked at, 
and the tabulator rejected it as an overvote, do not reinsert the ballot and override. Put 
this ballot aside for hand counting of all races. Because the tabulator rejected the ballot, 
no votes were counted. The intent of the voter is to vote for William Johnson and that 
   
 
29 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
intent should be given effect. In Example 5, one vote is counted for Thomas Hooker, John 
Winthrop, Silas Deane, Tapping Reeve, Jonathan Trumbull, and William Johnson.

D. Marking of Party Designation 
Any marking of a party designation printed on an absentee ballot must be disregarded in 
determining the elector’s intent when counting the votes. The tabulator will not recognize 
any markings outside the voting ovals . Similarly, in hand counting absentee ballots, 
marking of party designation must also be ignored and the ballot shall be treated as 
though it contains no improper marking. On a regular absentee ballot, if the elector circles 
the party designation, checks, underlines, or similarly marks in any manner, or write s in 
the party designation, no vote shall be deemed cast or canceled for any candidate due to 
such marking or writing. In Example 6 , the circle around “Republican” is ignored and a 
vote is counted for Sophocles, Pythagoras, and Socrates.

E. Vote on the Questions 
To vote on the question in an election, the elector must mark the “Yes” or “No” oval 
corresponding to each question on which the elector wishes to vote.  There are no 
questions on the ballot in a primary. Example 7 provides a brief illustration of what a vote 
on the question may look like.

F. Marking Errors: Determining Voter Intent 
Absentee voters will not have had the opportunity for the personal instruction that is 
available to electors at the polls. Absentee voters also will not be present to correct their 
errors if the tabulator rejects their ballot. Consequently, as previously mentioned, 
absentee ballot counters should give absentee ballots a quick look before they are fed 
into the tabulator in an attempt to catch marking errors which clearly indicate voter intent 
but may not be read by the tabulator. Absentee ballots  with such errors should be set 
aside for hand counting. If an absentee ballot counter has any doubt about whether an 
absentee ballot will be properly read by the tabulator due to a stray mark, marking 
candidate’s names with an “X”, checkmark, or circle, or incorrect ink being used, put the 
ballot aside for hand counting. 
  
   
 
31 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
G. Official Blank Absentee Ballots 
Electors who use the Official Blank Absentee Ballot cast their vote by writing in the names 
of specific candidates and offices for which they are voting. The elector may write the 
name of a candidate whose name will appear on the ballot, or the name of a registered 
write-in candidate in an election. These ballots must be hand counted. In Example 8, a 
vote is counted for Oliver Twist, Mark Twain, Rhett Butler, Miss Marple, and Will Rogers. 
Example 8 
Official Blank Absentee Ballot 
 
I hereby vote for Oliver Twist          for the office of  First Selectman         
and for Mark Twain                           for the office of  Selectman                              
and for Rhett Butler                          for the office of  Board of Education 
and for Miss Marple                       _ for the office of  Board of Education 
and for Will Rogers                                       for the office of  Board of Education 
 
In an election, electors who use the Official Blank Absentee Ballot or Federal Write -in 
Absentee Ballot (FWAB) and, in casting their vote, write in the name of the political party 
rather than the name of the specific candidate and write in the specific office  shall have 
their ballots counted because the intent of the voter governs. In Example 9, a vote is 
counted for the Democratic candidate for each office. 
Example 9 
OFFICIAL BLANK ABSENTEE BALLOT 
 
I hereby vote for Democratic         for the office of  First Selectman         
and for Democratic                           for the office of  Selectman                     
and for Democratic                         for the office of  Board of Education 
and for Democratic                         for the office of  Board of Education 
and for Democratic                         for the office of  Board of Education 
 
In Example 10, a vote is counted only for the Republican candidate for First Selectman 
and not for other offices. 
Example 10 
OFFICIAL BLANK ABSENTEE BALLOT 
 
I hereby vote for  Republican           for the office of  First Selectman    
and for _____________________    for the office of  Selectman          
and for _____________________   for the office of  Board of Education 
and for _____________________   for the office of  Board of Education 
and for _____________________   for the office of  Board of Education 
 
   
 
32 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
In Example 11, the intent of the voter governs , and a vote is counted for the Democratic 
candidate for each of the offices to be elected at the election. 
Example 11 
 Official Blank Absentee Ballot 
 
I hereby vote for Democratic Party    for the office of ___________________________                                             
and for                for all offices          for the office of ___________________________                                             
and for                                                 for the office of ___________________________                                             
and for                                                for the office of ___________________________                                             
and for                                                for the office of ___________________________                                                                
 
If on the Official Blank Absentee Ballot or Federal Write -in Absentee Ballot (FWAB) an 
elector writes in only the name of a candidate for President of the United States , a vote 
is counted for the Electors of President and Vice President representing said candidate 
and his Vice-Presidential running mate. In Example 12, a vote is counted for each of the 
Presidential and Vice-Presidential Electors representing John Doe and their running mate. 
Example 12 
Official Blank Absentee Ballot 
 
I hereby vote for     John Doe                for the office of    President       
 
H. Counting Absentee Votes When a Vacancy has Occurred 
Special rules apply for counting absentee votes cast when a candidate has withdrawn or 
dies. (Secs. 9-428, 9-460) 
 
If a candidate dies within 24 hours of the opening of the polls on election day, the 
candidate’s name is not withdrawn and votes for that candidate are counted. If the 
deceased candidate receives a plurality of the votes, a vacancy shall exist in the office and 
shall then be filled later in the manner prescribed by law. (Sec. 9-460) 
 
If a candidate in an election or party -endorsed candidate in a primary dies more than 24 
hours before the opening of polls, or if a candidate withdraws their candidacy, they may 
be replaced by a new candidate. The absentee ballot may show the replacement 
candidate’s name, or it may show the former candidate’s name, depending on the date 
of the withdrawal.  
 
If the registrar of voters has notified election officials that a candidate’s name has 
changed since the first absentee ballot was issued and that the voting tabulator has been 
programmed not to count that office on the absentee ballots, the absentee ballot should 
be hand counted for that office only before it is fed into the tabulator (see Step 10D). 
 
If the ballot shows the former candidate’s name, a vote cast for the former candidate is 
not counted as a vote cast for the replacement candidate.  However, if the ballot shows 
   
 
33 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
the name of the new candidate, a vote cast for the new candidate is counted. Additionally, 
if an elector writes in the name of the new candidate, the write-in vote is counted. 
Step 13. Filling out the Forms 
A. Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count 
When the count is complete, the moderator must record the total ballots counted and 
rejected for each  count and sign the form titled “Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count” 
located at the end of this Manual as part of the Moderator’s Return.  
 
B. Tally Sheets 
When absentee ballots are counted in the polls, the moderator must fill out all entries  
relating to hand counted absentee ballots on the form titled “Tally Sheet” of this Manual 
as part of the Moderator’s Return. The moderator must also fill out the form titled  
“Certificate of Number Checked as Voting by Absentee Ballot” of this Manual as part of 
the Moderator’s Return. 
 
C. Moderator’s Record of Hand Counted Absentee Ballot Count for Candidates 
Note: This is a separate record  that should be sealed in depository envelopes with the 
hand counted absentee ballots. 
 
The moderator must complete this  separate record of the number of votes cast by 
absentee ballot for each candidate. The moderator may use the form titled “Moderator’s 
Record of Hand Counted Absentee Ballot Count for Candidates” located at the end of this 
Manual, or the moderator may use a tally sheet which clearly shows the n ame of each 
candidate and the votes cast by absentee ballot for each candidate. A separate form must 
be completed for each voting district or for each time of counting if central counting is 
taking place. 
 
The absentee ballot counters must write the date, the time of the count (if central 
counting), and sign their names on all notes, worksheets, and other written materials 
used in the absentee counting  procedure. These all must be sealed in the depository 
envelope with the hand counted absentee ballots. (Sec. 9-150a(m)) 
 
D. Certificate of Write-in Ballots for Registered Write-in Candidates 
Write-in votes cast by absentee ballot for registered write-in candidates must be recorded 
on the form titled “Certificate of Write -in Votes for Registered Write -in Candidates” as 
part of the Moderator’s Return. 
Step 14. Depository Envelopes 
A. Hand Counted Absentee Ballots 
The absentee ballot counters must place into the depository envelopes: the hand counted 
absentee ballots; all notes, worksheets, and other written materials used in the counting 
procedures; and the form titled  “Moderator’s Record of Absentee Ballot Count for 
Candidates.”    
   
 
34 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
 
The depository envelope must be sealed sideways and lengthwise with nonreusable tape. 
The tape must cover the envelope flap. Each depository envelope shall be marked with 
the time of the count  (if central counting) and the voting district, in towns divided into 
voting districts. Additionally, the absentee ballot counters should endorse their names 
and the contents of the depository envelope on the depository envelope. (Secs. 9-150a(f), 
9-150a(m)) 
 
B. Empty and “Rejected” Envelopes 
In the same manner  described in Step 14A , prepare depository envelopes containing 
envelopes either discarded or marked “Rejected.” 
 
C. Delivery of Depository Envelopes to the Moderator 
The absentee ballot counters must give the depository envelopes to the moderator. The 
moderator will then deliver all depository envelopes to the registrar of voters who will 
file all absentee ballot materials with the municipal clerk after the 14 -day lockdown 
period. (Secs. 9-150a(f), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 
10, 9-150a(m), as amended by P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 10) 
 
Additionally, the moderator will deliver to the registrar of voters the depository envelope 
containing absentee ballots labeled “Rejected as  an Absentee Ballot” that are to be 
considered Provisional Ballots. The moderator shall complete the form titled “Affidavit of 
Delivery and Receipt of Provisional Ballots.”  
 
 
  
   
 
35 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Central Counting Procedures 
Overview 
This section outlines and describe s the process for counting absentee ballots at one central 
location. Registrars of voters may choose to conduct the counting of absentee ballots at one 
central location rather than at individual polling places. 
Delivering Tabulator and Absentee Ballots to Counting Place 
When conducting the count of absentee ballots at one central counting location, election officials 
shall appear before the registrars  of voters to: receive the tabulator and absentee ballots for 
counting; examine the label on the tabulator; and sign a receipt and deliver it to the central 
counting moderator at the central counting place at the time(s) prescribed by the  registrar of 
voters. 
The central counting moderator shall sign two copies of the form titled “Central Counting 
Moderator’s Receipt for Tabulator .” The central counting moderator shall give the election 
officials one receipt and keep the other one. The officials and central counting moderator shall 
record the number on the tabulator’s seal on the form titled “Certificate of Preparation of 
Machine for Central Absentee Ballot Counting.” The officials shall then prepare the tabulator in 
accordance with the certificate. (Regs. 9-242a-9)   
Central Counting 
Tabulator-counted Absentee Ballots 
The procedure for counting absentee ballots at a central location should be as similar as possible 
to the procedure for counting absentee ballots at the individual polling places. The absentee 
ballots arrive from the registrars of voters separated into voting districts. The envelopes are 
checked, opened, and ballots to be hand counted are set aside. The tabulator-counted absentee 
ballots are fed into the tabulator by districts. Because absentee ballots can be counted at a time 
prescribed by the registrar of voters, the votes on the tabulator-counted absentee ballots are not 
known until after 8 p.m. The empty envelopes and absentee ballots marked as “Rejected” are to 
be kept and sealed separately by voting district in depository envelopes labeled “Tabulator  
Count—Empty Envelopes—Voting District #__.” 
 The tabulators will no longer sort the Write -In ballots; however, they will present the image of 
any Write-In votes in the report so that the counters may hand count and record them. 
  
   
 
36 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Hand Counted Absentee Ballots 
The hand counted absentee ballots are recorded on the tally sheets and are sealed by voting 
districts in to depository envelope s labeled “Hand Counted Absentee Ballots District #__ ,” 
together with the separate form titled “Moderator’s Record o f Hand Counted Absentee Ballot 
Count for Candidates.” 
Security of Tabulator and Depository Envelopes 
The central counting moderator shall certify the public counter number and the seal number on 
the tabulator, and the date and time after each count on the  form titled  “Central Counting 
Moderator’s Receipt for Tabulator.” The central counting moderator and registrars shall sign the 
certification, and the central counting moderator shall retain the certificate until the close of the 
polls. The registrars shall ensure that the tabulator and all sealed and labeled depository 
envelopes of absentee ballots remain under direct supervision of election officials for the entire 
day. (Reg. 9-242a-25) 
Checking off Absentee Electors  
Ballots may be counted only after the names of absentee electors have been checked off the 
official checklist in use at the polling places. This may be accomplished by a telephone call 
between the central counting moderator and the checkers at each polling place. The counting 
then proceeds as outlined in the previous section titled “ Procedure for Counting Absentee 
Ballots.” (Sec. 9-140c(i), as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 7) 
After all ballots are counted, ballot totals are printed out in accordance with the procedures on 
the form titled “Certificate of Ending Central Absentee Ballot Counting” , then transferred to the 
Tally Sheet for the proper voting district and the tabulator printout is signed and attached to the 
Tally Sheets. Two additional duplicate Election Results Reports are printed out, added to the hand 
counted absentee ballot results, announced, and the returns are then filed as prescribed in the 
“Certificate of Ending Central Absentee Ballot Counting.”  
Tabulator Counted Absentee Ballots from  All Voting Districts Mixed 
Together 
When absentee ballots are counted at a central place, the tabulator -counted absentee ballots 
from all voting districts end up in the ballot transfer case for each count all mixed together. 
  
   
 
37 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Moderator Log 
The Moderator must now keep a log of all rejected absentee ballots for the following reasons:   
 1. Failure to sign the voter certification that is on the envelope. 
 2. Failure of asterisk* voters to provide appropriate HAVA identification.   
Moderators must keep a log of applicants whose ballots were rejected for one of the reasons 
above. The log must include the specific reason each applicant’s ballot was rejected. The log must 
then be sent to the Office of the Secretary of the State with the voting results. (P.A. 26-1 Sec. 90) 
Completion of Moderator’s Return 
The central counting moderator must be furnished with a Moderator’s Return for each voting  
district within the town. The central counting moderator must complete the following items on 
each return: 
1. Central Counting Moderator’s Statement of Receipt 
2. Central Counting Moderator’s Receipt for Tabulator 
3. Certificate of Preparation of Machine for Central Absentee Ballot Counting 
4. Certificate of Absentee Ballot Count (for each voting district) 
5. Absentee Ballot Tally Sheets (for each voting district) 
6. Write-in Votes for Registered Write-in Candidates (for each voting district) 
7. Certificate of Ending Central Absentee Ballot Counting 
8. Certificate of Number Checked as Voting by Absentee Ballot (for each voting district) 
The central counting moderator adds only those votes cast as  absentee ballots and completes 
the absentee ballot returns  for each voting district.  It is the head moderator  who adds the 
absentee ballot returns to the returns for in-person voting.   
The central counting moderator alone is required to sign the forms correlating to points 1, 4, and 
6 above. The central counting moderator must also deliver the Central Returns for each voting 
district to the head moderator , who will attach the polling place Moderator’s Return to the 
central return for the applicable voting district. 
As a reminder, the central counting moderator has already completed a separate  record of the 
number of hand counted votes cast by absentee ballot for each candidate as outlined in Step 
13C. The record of hand counted votes cast by absentee ballot for each candidate should utilize 
either the suggested form s, or a different tally sheet which clearly shows the name of each 
candidate and the votes cast by absentee ballot for each candidate. The tally sheet shall be sealed 
in the depository envelope with the hand counted absentee ballots for each voting district and 
each count. 
 
 
   
 
38 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Head Moderator’s Total of Central and Polling Place Returns 
The head moderator is required to add the tabulator total recorded on each of the polling place 
returns to the absentee ballot count recorded on the central returns, district -by-district. The 
procedure to add the counts together involves: 
1. Record the cumulative “in -person” total for each candidate and question contained on 
each polling place return. 
2. Enter the absentee totals for each candidate as recorded on the Tally Sheet of the central 
counting return for each voting district. 
3. Add the totals of 1 and 2 together and enter that total for each candidate and question 
on a townwide. 
The district-by-district absentee ballot totals should never be recorded on the polling place form 
of the Moderator’s Return. (Secs. 9-307, 9-312, 9-440) 
Challenges, Canvasses, and Retention of Materials 
Absentee Ballot Challenges 
Any elector may challenge an absentee ballot. Challenges to absentee ballots must be in writing, 
under oath, and may only be made on the following grounds: 
1. False identity 
2. Disenfranchisement for conviction of a felony 
3. Lack of bona fide residence in the town and political subdivision hol ding the election or 
primary 
Absentee ballot challenges made after the distribution of ballots to the polling places or central 
counting locations must be made in writing to the moderator where the ballot is to be counted. 
The moderator (or a designated polling place official)  must deliver to the registrars the written 
challenge, the ballot contained inside the unopened outer and inner envelopes , and any other 
evidence relevant to the challenge. The registrars must sign a receipt for these materials.  
The registrar(s) will then decide to deny or uphold the challenge  and notify the moderator of 
their decision in writing. If the challenge is denied, the ballot must be counted. If the challenge is 
upheld, the ballot should by marked as “Rejected”  followed by the reasoning for the rejection. 
The “Rejected” ballot shall then be sealed in the depository envelope with other rejected 
absentee ballots. (Sec. 9-159p, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 20) 
Canvass of Absentee Ballots at Closing of Polls 
All absentee ballots received before the close of the polls must be counted. When the absentee 
ballot count and canvass of the tabulators have been completed, the moderator must complete 
the appropriate Moderator’s Return. 
 
   
 
39 Absentee Ballot Counting Manual July 2026  
Retention of Absentee Ballots and Materials 
The municipal clerk must retain the sealed depository envelopes containing the envelopes and 
rejected absentee ballots for 60 days after the election. If no contest is pending, the clerk  shall 
destroy the depository envelopes and their contents . The municipal clerk must also retain t he 
depository envelopes containing the ballots, the absentee ballot counters’ notes and worksheets, 
and the Moderator’s Record of Absentee Ballot Count for 180 days after the election. If no 
contest is pending, the clerk shall destroy the depository envelopes and their contents. Note: In 
the case of primaries or elections for federal offices, these materials are retained for 22 months. 
(Sec. 9-150b, as amended by P.A. 26-42 Sec. 11) 
Additionally, each municipality, through the Town Clerk, shall retain the video recording for each 
absentee ballot secure drop box  location beginning on the first day o f issuing absentee voting 
sets until the last retrieval of absentee ballots from that drop box location on election or primary 
day. Each recording shall be retained for up to 12 months and may then be destroyed if no contest 
or investigation is pending. (Sec. 9 -140b, as amended by P.A. 26 -1 Sec. 88, as amended by P.A. 
26-42 Sec. 6)
